- [x] RANE right-deck playback routing: AHHH reaches USB output 3/4, physically approved (2026-08-29)
  - Selected from Karl's physical report that ScratchLab's AHHH was controlled by the right platter/right upfader and reached master, but the RANE's LEFT channel meter LEDs illuminated.
  - Root cause, proven by an on-device requested-versus-granted diagnostic, not inferred: the RANE ONE MKII exposes **10** USB output channels, while `IOScratchPlaybackEngine` hardcoded `raneOutputChannelCount = 14` and gated the entire channel-map path on `session.maximumOutputNumberOfChannels >= 14`. `10 >= 14` is false, so ScratchLab requested plain stereo, `outputPairStartIndex` was nil, and no channel map was ever installed — playback fell through to the device's first output pair, 1/2, which is the LEFT deck. The pair index (`2` → physical 3/4) was correct the whole time; the gate meant it was never used. This is why BOTH the earlier 13/14 assumption and the later 3/4 value produced a wrong-deck meter: neither constant was ever applied.
  - `AVAudioSession` was NOT at fault and was not changed. The diagnostic showed it granting 10 of 10 channels on request; `options=8` decodes to `.defaultToSpeaker` alone, which did not block multichannel USB output. No category/mode/option change was made.
  - Implemented: a named, testable `RanePlaybackRoutingPolicy` in the already-shared `DVSHardwareProfile.swift` (both targets, Foundation-only, so no `project.pbxproj` reference was needed) with `rightDeckPairStartIndex = 2` and `minimumRequiredOutputChannels = 4` DERIVED from the pair rather than guessed. The engine now requests the connected route's actual `maximumOutputNumberOfChannels`, decides from the granted session count AND the output node's own channel count, builds the map sized to the real destination count, applies it, then READS IT BACK and verifies it still places the renderer on 2/3 before allowing playback. A recognized RANE that cannot be honoured now throws `IOScratchPlaybackRoutingError` and refuses to play, instead of silently degrading to stereo 1/2 — the exact fallback that put AHHH on the left deck. Non-RANE routes keep the ordinary stereo graph unchanged. DEBUG diagnostics consolidated to one `[RANE-ROUTING]` line; no Release logging added.
  - Replaced the brittle source-string test that merely searched for `let raneOutputPairStartIndex = 2`. That test passed throughout the entire bug, because the constant was never the wrong value. 17 behavioural tests now cover: 10-output accepted, 4-output accepted, 14-output still works, fewer than four rejected rather than routed to 1/2, output-node shortfall rejected even when the session grants enough, renderer mapped only to destination 2/3, left pair explicitly silenced, master pair never targeted, read-back rejecting left-deck/swapped/nil maps, non-RANE routes (including multichannel) remaining ordinary stereo, DVS input 3/4 unchanged and independent, and right-upfader/right-deck-crossfader ownership unchanged.
  - DVS input remains physical 3/4 in its own Core Audio input namespace. Input and output both legitimately use 3/4; neither value may be derived from the other, and the policy documents that explicitly.
  - PHYSICALLY APPROVED by Karl on iPhone K + RANE ONE MKII: AHHH plays through the right deck, only the right meter responds, right upfader and right-deck crossfader control it, master output is normal, DVS unaffected. On-device log corroborates exactly: `granted=10 max=10 outputNode=10 map=[-1,-1,0,1,-1,-1,-1,-1,-1,-1] onRightDeck=true decision=raneRightDeck`, unchanged at `after-engine-start`.
  - Verification: `RanePlaybackRoutingPolicyTests` 17/17 twice; `MIDIUserMixerGainTests` 39/39, `MIDIFaderCurveTests` 22/22, `DVSControlVinylAnalyzerTests` 40/40, `MIDIPlatterContinuousDriveTests` 17/17, plus all Slice A/B/C suites green; iOS build and macOS `build-for-testing` succeeded; fixtures 47/47; `git diff --check` clean. `scripts/build.sh all` exit 65, 3090 tests / 49 skipped / 11 failures — failing-name set identical to the post-restore baseline (9 names), count up by exactly 17.
  - Standalone-test isolation cleanup completed 2026-08-29: `PlatterTestSampleLoadTests` now passes an explicit app-bundle resource root, and `MIDILearnEngineTests` uses a unique, reset-per-test `UserDefaults` suite injected into the MIDI selection/legacy crossfader persistence boundary. Both suites pass standalone twice and inside the full test plan without changing production audio, routing, MIDI, or sample behavior.
- [x] Capture-integrity Slice A: shared capture-evidence model so controller motion is not reported missing (2026-08-29)
  - Selected from Karl's physical iPhone take-002 evidence, where live blue notation responded while the HUD read `MOTION Motion Missing` and Review simultaneously showed `Ready to keep`, `Motion pending`, `Motion Missing`, and `Fader: No movement`.
  - Root cause: three independent sites derived motion presence solely from a linked Apple Watch artifact — `CompanionCameraView.handleFinishedRecording`, the export-package builder in the same file, and `SessionExportCoordinator`'s recovery path. The take's real platter/fader evidence was already decoded and persisted into the sidecar's `detectedNotation` immediately beforehand, then ignored by every presence check. A valid RANE take with no Watch paired therefore reported motion missing and exported as motionless.
  - Implemented: added a shared, domain-only `CaptureMotionEvidence` in `CaptureCore` (platter / fader-count / watch / DVS) plus `CaptureMotionEvidenceResolver`, which all three sites now resolve through, reading the same persisted sidecar notation so a live take and a recovered take can never disagree. A scratch gesture is discriminated from a free-running powered platter structurally, by the presence of a decoded reversal, reusing `derivePlatterMovementEvents`' existing noise gates rather than adding a second threshold layer. Crossfader activity is recorded but deliberately never substitutes for platter movement. DVS is modelled as `.unsupported` because iOS capture has no timecode-pipeline feed at finalization today, and it never contributes presence.
  - Export validation was made source-aware in the same slice: two gates (`packageValidationIssues` and the canonical-context builder) previously required a linked Watch artifact whenever `motionPresent == true`, which would have rejected — and in the canonical path hard-thrown on — every newly-truthful controller-only take. `SessionExportTake.motionSources` is additive and optional; `nil` preserves the historical Watch-only behaviour for pre-existing callers and fixtures. `SessionExportTake` is an in-memory input struct, so there is no on-disk export-schema change.
  - Verification: 13 new `CaptureMotionEvidenceTests` passed twice; `ControllerPlatterDecoderTests` 14/14, `CaptureRecoveryPhase2CoreTests` 58/58, `ScratchLabNotationAndExportTests` 29/29, `Take002ControllerRoundTripTests` 3/3 (1 skipped); isolated macOS build, macOS `build-for-testing`, and generic iOS Simulator build all succeeded; `python3 scripts/test_capture_pipeline.py` 47/47; `git diff --check` clean. `scripts/build.sh all` was run once and exited on the pre-existing dirty-WIP baseline only — see DEV_LOG for the proof that no failing suite references any Slice A symbol.
  - Not claimed by this slice: the hardcoded `"Ready to keep"` literal and the resolver's two-strings-for-one-false-input shape both survive until Slice B, so a genuinely motionless take can still render contradictory labels. Nothing was committed or pushed.
- [x] Capture-integrity Slice B: truthful review state derivation (2026-08-29)
  - Root cause: the operator message was a hardcoded `"Ready to keep"` literal decided at two call sites in `CompanionCameraView.swift` (the finish handler and `applyAudioPresence`) with no motion input at all, while `GuidedCaptureReviewStateResolver.motionAssessment` computed `syncStatus` and `motionStatusTitle` as two independent strings from the same inputs — deliberately returning `"Motion pending"` and `"Motion Missing"` for one false `motionPresent`. `CaptureReview` then stored all four as separately-settable `var`s, so nothing structurally prevented the take-002 combination.
  - Implemented: one `GuidedCaptureReviewState` (typed `CaptureReviewReadiness` + `CaptureMotionStatus`) resolved by `GuidedCaptureReviewStateResolver.reviewState(...)`, which now also folds in `recordingFailed` and `duration` so readiness can no longer be decided at the call site. `operatorMessage`, `syncStatus`, `motionStatusTitle`, and `motionPresent` became computed projections of that single value on `CaptureReview`; `syncStatus` restates the motion wording verbatim when motion is the blocker instead of inventing a second vocabulary. Added `CaptureReviewReadiness.retakeRecommended` so a valid-but-motionless take reads honestly and stays keepable. Added a shared `CaptureMotionEvidencePresenter` producing `Platter: Present · MIDI` / `Fader: 4 events` / `Watch: Not used` rows, replacing the standalone Fader block in both the landscape and portrait review surfaces.
  - `"Motion pending"` no longer exists anywhere in the resolver, and two exhaustive-input tests assert that no combination of the seven inputs can produce it or pair `readyToKeep` with missing motion.
  - Verification: 10 `GuidedCaptureReviewStateTests` + 7 `CaptureMotionEvidencePresenterTests` + 13 Slice A `CaptureMotionEvidenceTests` all passed twice (30/30); `CaptureRecoveryPhase2CoreTests` 58/58, `ScratchLabNotationAndExportTests` 29/29, `ControllerPlatterDecoderTests` 14/14, `Take002ControllerRoundTripTests` 3 passed/1 skipped; isolated iOS + macOS builds and macOS `build-for-testing` green; fixtures 47/47; `git diff --check` clean. `scripts/build.sh all` ran once, exit 65, **3052 tests / 49 skipped / 15 failures** — the failing name set is byte-identical to the Slice A baseline, and all three new suites passed inside that run.
  - Not claimed: DVS is intentionally omitted from the evidence rows because no real feed exists; `Fader` still reads `No movement` on unmapped controllers until Slice C; finalization bounding is Slice E, not this slice, despite the earlier title wording. Nothing committed or pushed.
- [x] Capture-integrity Slice C: crossfader evidence provenance and take-window bounding (2026-08-29)
  - CORRECTION to this task's earlier wording: it previously claimed the `"Raw ch 15 · CC 8"` row "shows as configured while persisting zero events". That was wrong — `mappingDescription` already returned `"Not active · expected Raw ch 15 · CC 8"`, which was truthful. The claim was written without reading the function and is retracted here.
  - CORRECTION to the take-002 diagnosis: `ScratchLabApp.configureMIDILearnDevice()` already auto-applies the verified RANE mapping (crossfader CC8/ch15) at startup when a source resolves, so a `Fader: No movement` reading on a fader-open Baby Scratch is very likely CORRECT, not a capture bug. Slice C is a robustness and provenance fix; it must not be described as the fix for that screenshot.
  - Real defects fixed: (1) `MIDIActionResolver` resolved transport against the hardware registry but resolved crossfader only from a learned mapping, so manual device selection in Capture/Advanced — which, unlike app startup, never auto-applies the verified mapping — left a connected RANE recording zero fader evidence; (2) nothing bounded the END of the take window, so controller moves made between Stop and movie finalization were decoded into the finished take.
  - Implemented: `FaderMappingSource` (`learned` / `certifiedRegistry`); a certified-registry crossfader fallback in `MIDIActionResolver` gated on a real registry match at `.certified` confidence plus a non-diagnostic `.crossfader` binding, reusing `MIDIControlBinding.matches(_:)`; evidence-only enforcement so the registry path never calls `setCrossfaderPosition`; provenance persisted on `RawMixerMIDIEvent.mappingSource` (optional, backward-compatible decode) and carried into review rows and export metadata; a monotonic `markCaptureStopped()` bound in the `CACurrentMediaTime()` domain; and Hardware Setup wording distinguishing learned / certified default / not active.
  - macOS mapping unification is deliberately OUT of scope and documented as a known divergence in `MIDIActionResolver.swift`: macOS keeps a separate `CrossfaderCCMapping` in `UserDefaults` and still requires an explicit crossfader learn.
  - Not changed: `deriveDetectedNotationFaderEvents` and its `minimumValueDelta` gate are untouched, so an open-fader Baby Scratch still produces zero fader events and no click symbols.
- [x] Capture-integrity Slice D: split gesture-relative notation from sample-position tracking (2026-08-29)
  - Root cause: controller notation and audio sample travel consumed the same signed, unwrapped motor-phase coordinate. That coordinate is correct for the waveform/playhead, but rendering it directly made accumulated free-running revolutions become the baseline of the next scratch gesture.
  - Implemented explicit shared coordinate semantics: `gestureRelativeNotation` rebases every decoder-committed movement run to its own local origin while preserving signed direction, timestamps, and full excursion; `samplePosition` remains the raw signed displacement from the hot-cue origin. Live iOS notation, live macOS notation, macOS saved Review/Take Detail, and camera/overlay notation now use the same presentation projection. **Scope correction (2026-08-30): this slice delivers NO production iOS saved-take surface.** By platform — iOS: live signed playhead and live notation. macOS: live notation plus saved Review and Take Detail. Production iOS saved-take notation/playback/detail does not exist and is tracked separately below; do not read this slice as having shipped it. The existing movement-run/reversal decoder and its noise gates remain the only scratch boundary source.
  - The replacement iOS/macOS hardware captures and screenshots then exposed a presentation-only follow-up: ordinary controller scratches were about 0.08–0.22 revolution, but the renderer treated one revolution as a full lane and macOS constrained the live trace to 118 points. A fixed shared quarter-revolution full-scale display projection now makes 0.10 / 0.20 / 0.25 revolution occupy 40% / 80% / 100% of the lane, and the macOS live card fills the camera stage. Travel at or above 0.25 revolution intentionally reaches the display rail; exact physical excursion remains unchanged in the event and export. A camera-overlay endpoint defect that applied sub-full travel twice was also corrected.
  - Canonical movement evidence, scoring, export, and audio scheduling remain unchanged. The waveform playhead still consumes raw signed/unwrapped travel and can remain before cue or past the sample end. Presentation reconciliation expands fusion-merged same-direction spans to their covered decoder runs and appends decoder-valid raw runs omitted by fusion without mutating the canonical snapshot.
  - Verification: 147 focused tests passed twice; the 88-test relevant regression + saved-Review set passed twice; isolated macOS and generic iOS Simulator builds succeeded, including the embedded Watch target; fixtures passed 47/47; and both broad configured passes ran 3,111 XCTest cases / 49 skipped with the established 11 assertion failures across the identical 9 names, plus 366/366 Swift Testing cases green. `git diff --check` passed. No push was performed; this slice was committed as its own checkpoint.
  - Physical approval RECORDED (Karl, 2026-08-30), all three refreshed checks passed. macOS: free-spin did not accumulate into the next gesture's baseline; catch, unequal push/pull, and reversal direction displayed correctly; completed strokes remained fixed. macOS saved Review and Take Detail preserved stroke order, direction, timing, and relative excursion. iOS: playhead crossed `BEFORE START` and `PAST END` without wrapping and stayed synchronized with audible AHHH. The quarter-turn full-scale zoom is approved on this basis.
  - Corroborating macOS export `53d4b65d` (not itself the approval — visual acceptance came from Karl): 12 files, none zero-byte, three debug traces; one `slowDrag` free-spin of 0.8419 rev over 5.99s; 18 pulls from −0.0240 to −0.0558 rev and 7 pushes from 0.0273 to 0.0524 rev, so performed asymmetry survives into the evidence. Post-catch gestures still start at 0.7915–0.8507 in the export because canonical export/scoring retain the raw signed coordinate and rebasing is presentation-only — that is correct, not a regression.
- [x] Capture-integrity Slice E: one bounded, take-ID-scoped finalization state machine (2026-08-30)
  - Root shape of the defect: there was no single authoritative finalization state. "Are we saving", "did the watchdog already run", "was this summary already handled", and "is this summary for this take" were four facts stored in four places — a `flowState` case, a view-owned 12s + 3s `Task` watchdog, a `lastHandledRecordingID` string on the store, and a `matchesActiveSavingTake` helper checked independently by three delivery paths — so they could disagree. The de-duplication check ran at the END of the finish handler, after controller notation had been re-persisted, `pendingScratchAudioURL` consumed, and artifact banners raised; a duplicate recorder callback therefore repeated all of that and falsely reported the rendered AHHH audio missing.
  - Consolidated the existing partial repair (timer freeze, matching-summary handling, stop retry, recovery wait, preserved staging, Stop-during-start) into one pure `CaptureFinalizationMachine` in `CaptureCore.swift` — a file already in BOTH targets and Foundation-only, so no `project.pbxproj` change was needed and the policy is unit-testable from the macOS test target even though the driving view is iOS-target-only. One typed `CaptureFinalizationState` scoped to a `CaptureFinalizationTakeKey(sessionID:takeNumber:)`; `apply(_:at:)` is clock-free and returns an ordered effect list; `CaptureFinalizationBudget` states the 15s worst case (12s wait + one 3s retry) that two hardcoded sleeps used to imply. The old `finalizationWatchdog` task and `lastHandledRecordingID` are deleted, not supplemented — `CaptureFinalizationDeadlineScheduler` is the only place a deadline becomes elapsed time and scheduling replaces the previous timer, so a second watchdog has nowhere to come from.
  - Guarantees, each with a test: every accepted Stop reaches exactly one terminal result (Review or an explicit recoverable failure) inside the bound; duplicate recorder callbacks, republished summaries, and late deadline delivery return an empty effect list, so the finish handler gates on that BEFORE any side effect instead of de-duplicating afterwards; a summary differing in take number or session ID is refused and leaves the active take still waiting; Stop while the recorder is still starting emits an extra startup cancel alongside the ordinary recorder stop; the timer freezes at the FIRST accepted Stop because a later Stop emits nothing; every failure path preserves staged media BEFORE presenting the failure; phone Stop and Watch Stop call one `stopTake(source:)` and produce byte-identical effects and state. Optional audio inspection is bounded and now returns `nil` on timeout so a slow `AVAsset` open cannot downgrade a good take to `missingAudio`.
  - Test debt repaid: `testGuidedCaptureSavingFreezesElapsedTimeAtStopRequest` pinned the literal `"activeTake.stoppedAt = Date()"` rather than the freeze guarantee, and `testWatchStartStopControlsTheIPhoneCaptureStateMachine` pinned `"stopTake()"`. Both now assert the unified machine-driven path, with the behaviour itself covered by 28 new `CaptureFinalizationMachineTests` plus four Slice E source-structure guards.
  - Verification: `CaptureFinalizationMachineTests` 28/28 twice; Slice E source guards 7/7; regressions twice each (`CaptureRecoveryPhase2CoreTests` 58/58, the `GuidedCaptureReviewState`/`CaptureMotionEvidence`/`CaptureMotionEvidencePresenter`/`ControllerPlatterDecoder`/`RanePlaybackRoutingPolicy` set 123/123, and the notation/export set 45 executed / 1 skipped / 0 failures); isolated generic iOS Simulator, generic iOS device (embedded Watch), and macOS `build-for-testing` builds all green; fixtures 47/47; `git diff --check` clean.
  - Physical check PASSED 3/3 on iPhone `K` (2026-08-30) against a signed build of the current source with the embedded Watch app. E-1: Save Take froze the elapsed readout immediately and Review appeared. E-2: Stop tapped inside the start race produced the iOS `Ready to keep` Review screen with Keep / Retry / Discard, and the take was kept and exported — the **deferred-stop branch (a)**, i.e. `stopRequestedWhileRecordingStarts` retained the request and delivered it from `didStartRecordingTo`. E-3: a single Watch tap started and a single Watch tap stopped the take, phone Capture foreground and Watch showing Transfer Connected.
  - E-2 is explicitly NOT the preservation-timeout branch: `preserveStagedMedia` → `presentRecoverableFailure` never ran and the 15s bound was never reached. The timeout branch therefore remains covered by automated tests only, not by hardware.
  - Supplied iOS export verified healthy and complete (session `b5a93fd7`): one 11s take, `recordingStatus: completed`, no `errorDescription`, 35 movement events, 9,572 mixer MIDI events, confidence 0.948, `watchSyncState: acknowledged`, real Watch CSV in the archive, and a full `take_allocated` → `recording_completed` → `notation_snapshot` → `watch_linked` audit trail. An earlier session's reviewed-but-not-kept take was also confirmed to have completed finalization, so its absence from that export was correct behaviour.
  - Not claimed by this slice: production iOS has no saved-take playback/detail surface, so a kept take cannot be reopened or played back on iPhone. That is a separate unchecked product task below, not a Slice E defect. No push was performed; this slice was committed as its own isolated checkpoint.

- [x] MIDI Learn stale-value fix + Rane ONE MKII mapping-failure root cause (2026-08-28)
  - Rane ONE MKII "left/right upfaders and hot cues never map" traced to the controller's physical channel-assign switch above the fader, not a CoreMIDI input-path defect: with that switch not fully left the mixer section transmits no MIDI at all; with it fully left every control transmits and its address matches the existing verified-mapping assumptions.
  - Verified MIDI tuples (single CoreMIDI source endpoint, switch fully left; 1-indexed channel then raw 0-indexed): crossfader `CC 8 ch 16 (raw 15)` value 0–127; left upfader `CC 28 ch 1 (raw 0)` value 0–127; right upfader `CC 28 ch 2 (raw 1)` value 0–127; Hot Cue 1 `NoteOn ch 6 (raw 5) note 20 vel 127`; Hot Cue 2 `NoteOn ch 6 (raw 5) note 21 vel 127`; SHIFT + Hot Cue 1 `NoteOn ch 16 (raw 15) note 50`.
  - MIDI Learn no longer displays or captures a stale pre-Learn value: the panel shows "move the control now" until an event arrives after Learn begins, driven by a session-scoped observed-value field cleared on session start / cancel / monitor reset (previously it parsed the persistent last-CC string, which held the parked crossfader value).
  - While learning a continuous control, a CC already mapped to a different continuous action (e.g. the still-streaming crossfader) is ignored, so cross-mapped controller activity cannot be captured as the control being learned; the session stays open for the real control. Hot-cue-via-CC learn is unaffected.
  - Six regression tests added to `MIDILearnEngineTests` and passing: observed value nil until a fresh event, reset on a new session, cleared on cancel, hot-cue note-learn surfaces the note number, "Learn Right Upfader" ignores the streaming crossfader CC8 then learns the real CC28, and the guard does not block a non-colliding CC. ScratchLabDesktop build + build-for-testing succeeded; 113/113 MIDI-suite tests pass.
  - Scope: no broader MIDI work is complete — the in-flight verified-mapping/registry layer and iOS coordinator wiring remain WIP. Committed as `ce12fe0` (fixes + tests) and `e9fd591` (DEV_LOG entry); not pushed.

Rather than forcing the full iOS/macOS-oriented file (materials, StatusBadge/Chip/card/button components sized for phone/desktop) onto the Watch target, extracted the minimal platform-safe subset actually needed — the `Color(scratchLabHex:)` initializer plus 12 semantic color constants — into a new file, `ScratchLab/DesignSystem/ScratchLabCoreColors.swift`, added to all three targets (`ScratchLab`, `ScratchLabDesktop`, `ScratchLabWatch`) via `project.pbxproj`. This exact "small pure-Swift file shared across `ScratchLab`+`ScratchLabWatch`" pattern already existed in the project for `WatchMotionCapture.swift`/`CaptureReliability.swift` — the new file follows the same precedent (1 file reference, 1 group listing, 3 build-file entries — one per target's Sources phase). `ScratchLabDesignSystem.swift`'s `Sem.accent/success/warning/danger/info/textPrimary/textSecondary/textTertiary` and `Surface.canvas/raised` now resolve through `ScratchLabCoreColor`'s identical hex values instead of re-declaring them, so there is exactly one canonical value per overlapping role, not two independently-maintained palettes. `pbxproj` diff is narrow and additive: one new `PBXFileReference`, three new `PBXBuildFile` entries, one group-child addition, three Sources-phase-listing additions — no existing entry was changed or removed.
  - Migrated `WatchCaptureView.swift` onto `ScratchLabCoreColor.*`: root background (2-stop black/blue gradient → the same canvas/raised gradient shape used everywhere else in the app), the Ready/Recording status label (was cyan/red → now `.success`/`.danger`, since "Ready" and "Recording" are genuine readiness/recording states, distinct from the Start/Stop button's own accent/danger role as the primary action), the Start/Stop button (cyan/red → `.accent`/`.danger`), and the Connection/Transfer info rows (previously uncolored plain text → `.info` when connected/reachable, `.textTertiary` when not, matching "connected but not ready: informational" / "searching or waiting: neutral"). Added a small, purely presentational `WatchTransferStatusTone` classifier that maps `WatchMotionRecorder.transferStatus`'s existing, unmodified strings (e.g. "Sent to your paired device.", "Couldn't send to your paired device. Will retry automatically.", "Pair your watch with your device to send sessions.") to success/warning/danger/info/neutral tones — an unrecognized string safely falls back to neutral rather than guessing. - [x] iOS MIDI Phase 2 — shared packet framing + CoreMIDI transport readiness

It does not control platters, faders, playback, calibration, or MIDI Learn.
  - Added evidence-only `MIDIReadinessState`: no connected source = unavailable; connection alone = deviceConnected; first valid supported message = receivingMessages. DEBUG discovery logs are de-duplicated and message logs are throttled to once per second.

  - Added `StereoPair.resolveSelection(availablePairs:remembered:)` (default-first / restore-remembered / safe-nil-fallback) and `StereoPair.extractInterleaved(_:channelCount:)` (pure, testable channel extraction) to `AudioHardwareRouteState`.
  - `AudioEngine` now extracts the selected stereo pair from the raw PCM buffer *before* downmixing (`extractStereoPair(from:pair:)`), instead of always downmixing every channel. A 14-channel RANE-style device selecting "3/4" now reads exactly hardware indices 2/3; mono/stereo mic behavior is unchanged (verified: falls back to the prior full-downmix path when no pair is selected).
  - DEBUG-only "Audio Route Changed" diagnostics moved into the adapter, gated on actual metadata change only.
  - Added 8 new tests (selection defaulting/remembering/fallback, interleaved extraction incl. the RANE 3/4 example, malformed-input safety). All 18 tests passed on the macOS test host.

It publishes the active input's name, UID, transport, sample rate, channels, stereo pairs, PCM callback activity, and signal level into `AudioHardwareRouteState` using `AVAudioSession.availableInputs`, the active port/channel metadata, and `AVAudioInputNode.inputFormat(forBus:)`.

- [ ] Capture-movement loss — Phase 1 (per-observation trace + diagnostics export) and Phase 2 (exact replay), evidence-only; no sampling/threshold fix yet
  - Selected because: the 2026-08-13 Take 006 session (176 raw platter samples @ ~13.35 Hz, 35 audio events, only 4 `recordMovementEvents`) proved movement is lost in `HandDirectionTracker → RoutineDetectedNotationBuilder → RoutineNotationEventNormalizer → RoutineNotationFusionEngine`, but the existing export only carried the normalised platter timeline, which cannot reconstruct raw X/Y, joint confidence, Vision misses, continuity substitutions, publication timing, or MainActor delay — so the exact loss stage could not be proven offline. Karl directed: implement evidence capture + exact replay FIRST, do not change sampling or thresholds until the exported/replayed evidence identifies the cause and a failing ground-truth test covers the fix.
  - Implemented (all DEBUG-only; Release byte-stable) in `ScratchLabDesktop/Services/MacCaptureEngine.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktop/Views/MacAnalyzerView.swift`, `ScratchLab/Services/SessionExportCoordinator.swift`: a versioned `debug/take-N_movement_trace.json` (every chronological observation: presentation/processing/Vision-completion + take-relative timestamps, point kind real/continuityFill/miss/jumpRejected, raw + smoothed point, joint confidence, raw direction, idle reason, tracker confidence, semantic direction, dedup outcome, and the exact builder input) plus `debug/take-N_movement_diagnostics.json` (the completed `RoutineMovementDiagnosticsSnapshot`, now `Codable`, with all drop-reason dictionaries and final counts). Both are frozen at take finalization, associated to the take, copied into `debug/` with canonical `takeID`/`takeNumber` fix-up during package construction, written as deterministic sorted-key JSON, and surface encode/write failures (not silently swallowed). A `MovementTraceReplay.replay(...)` path consumes the trace directly through the REAL tracker → builder → normalizer → fusion pipeline (not the platter timeline), recomputing confidence/timing from the recorded inputs. `trackedHandPoint` now returns the winning joint's confidence.
  - Tests in `ScratchLabDesktopTests/CaptureReliabilityPhase1Tests.swift` (new `MovementTraceDiagnosticsTests`): trace export round-trips + chronological order; diagnostics export round-trips with drop-reason dicts + final counts; replay reproduces a single forward stroke (1 raw/normalized/final event, direction `backward` with increasing X); replay determinism. 4/4 pass.
  - Verification: new tests 4/4; `CaptureReliabilityPhase1CoreTests` 322 tests, 8 failures all pre-existing (iOS scheme/target membership and Coach resource-bundling checks in this macOS-only worktree — unchanged by this diff); `python3 scripts/test_capture_pipeline.py` 47/47; macOS `build` + `build-for-testing` succeeded; `git diff --check` clean. NOT claiming the capture problem is fixed — the next hardware take must produce the real trace so offline replay can be compared to the live diagnostics and the loss classified per stage (no Vision observation / low-confidence joint / discontinuity / impossible-jump / tracker-idle / reversal-not-committed / builder-fragment / normalizer-reject / fusion-reject / timestamp-ordering).
  - 2026-08-29 Take 009 evidence (`session_2026_08_29_platter_baby_scratch_95_bpm.zip`): live diagnostics contain 750 observations / 598 builder samples / 48 final movements, but exact replay produced 0 final movements. The first divergence was the builder (live 134 starts + 6 extensions; replay 64 starts + 76 extensions). Root cause is an evidence-wiring defect: live notation receives the angular `processed.position`, while the trace incorrectly persisted the smoothed image-space display point as `builderPoint`; brief misses also persisted a display point although live passed nil. Corrected the DEBUG trace to store the actual scalar builder position in `builderPoint.x`, and added an environment-gated real-take replay test that prints the live/ideal/exact funnel and requires the exact final count to match. Existing Take 009 cannot be repaired exactly because the required angular builder inputs were never stored; one new hardware take from this build remains required before this task can be marked complete. No detection threshold, sampling interval, Release capture behavior, or notation rule changed.

- [x] Harden macOS capture PCM extraction after click-assisted routine capture stack overflow
  - Selected because: the 2026-08-13 19:37 crash report identified `Thread stack size exceeded` on `scratchlab.mac.capture.audio`, inside `MacCaptureEngine.audioPacket(from:)` while an integer PCM conversion iterated a Range derived directly from `AudioBuffer.mDataByteSize`.
  - Implemented in `ScratchLabDesktop/Services/MacCaptureEngine.swift`: derive the exact conversion count from Core Media's authoritative frame count and the validated per-buffer channel layout; use byte size only to prove sufficient backing storage; reject zero, excessive, overflowing, short, or inconsistent packet metadata; replace the integer `Range.map` conversions with bounded loops; preserve Float32, Int16, Int32, interleaved downmix, and non-interleaved downmix behavior.
  - Tests in `ScratchLabDesktopTests/CaptureReliabilityPhase1Tests.swift`: oversized byte metadata stays bounded to frame count, insufficient/excessive metadata is rejected, a 14-channel Rane-style Int32 buffer downmixes correctly, and the existing non-interleaved Float32 regression remains green.
  - Verification: the four focused tests passed 4/4 in both configured test runs and the changed desktop target compiled. `scripts/build.sh` passed capture-pipeline fixtures 47/47; its broad desktop plan completed with established unrelated resource/scheme, MIDI, sample-playback, and existing dirty-notation failures, while none of the new audio-packet tests failed. Address Sanitizer compilation completed but the local test host was interrupted by repeated locked-device/CoreAudio service failures before producing a valid result bundle. Real click-assisted hardware capture remains the final crash-reproduction check.

(2) Corrected `closeForWriting()`'s doc comment, which incorrectly claimed a producer that raced past the `acceptingWritesFlag` check is "simply rejected" — not guaranteed. Documented precisely: calls that *observe* the closed flag are rejected; one already-entered producer call may still complete and publish one final slot; `stop()`'s barrier synchronizes consumer delivery only, not the external realtime producer; a future Slice 2 owner must disconnect/retire the producer callback and keep the ring alive until no producer can access it before releasing it; not currently a production hazard since Slice 1 isn't wired to live capture. No locks/allocation/dispatch/logging added to `publish()`.
  - Files (both untracked): `ScratchLabDesktop/Services/TimecodeRealtimeIngressRing.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/TimecodeRealtimeIngressRingTests.swift`. Plus the same surgical `project.pbxproj` addition as before (8 insertions: 2 `PBXBuildFile`, 2 `PBXFileReference`, 2 group, 2 Sources-phase — production file in `ScratchLabDesktop` Sources only, test file in `ScratchLabDesktopTests` Sources only; the 4 pre-existing `DVSOfflineGrainRendererTests` entries untouched). `MacCaptureEngine.swift` untouched.
  - What it is: the smallest independently-committable piece of the `AVAudioNode.installTap` replacement design — a preallocated, lock-free SPSC ring (`TimecodeRealtimeIngressRing`) plus a non-realtime draining worker (`TimecodeRealtimeIngressWorker`), proven in isolation with no `AVAudioEngine` graph change and no live production wiring.
  - **Review defects found and fixed this pass:**
    1. **Worker self-stop defect.** `tick()` checked `stopped` only once, before `drainPending` began; if the first consumer callback in a multi-slot drain called `worker.stop()`, the self-stop path (running on the worker's own queue) marked `stopped` and returned without a barrier, but `drainPending`'s loop kept delivering the remaining already-published slots to `consumer` — delivery after `stop()` had returned. Fixed: `tick()`'s `drainPending` closure now re-checks `stopped` before every delivery (`[weak self, consumer]`, `guard let self, !self.stopped...`), so a self-stop from inside the first callback silently no-ops every later slot in that same drain while still draining the ring. New regression: `testSelfStopFromFirstCallbackPreventsLaterSlotsInTheSameDrain` (publishes 3 slots, first callback calls `stop()`, proves exactly 1 delivery both immediately and after a further wait). Companion regression `testExternalThreadStopWaitsForAnInFlightCallbackToFinish` proves the pre-existing external-thread barrier guarantee still holds.
    2. **Configuration/allocation overflow.** `TimecodeRealtimeIngressRingConfiguration.init?` previously validated positivity only, then `Ring.init` performed unchecked `maximumChannelCount * maximumFrameCountPerSlot` and `slotCount * slotStride`, and `publish` converted `frameCount`/`channelCount` to `Int32` without proving the configured maxima fit. Fixed: `init?` now also validates both maxima fit `Int32`; computes `slotStride`/`sampleCapacity`/byte size via `multipliedReportingOverflow`, rejecting any overflow; and enforces a new documented `maximumSampleStorageByteSize` (256 MiB) so a technically non-overflowing but absurd configuration (e.g. 1,000 slots × 1,000 channels × 100,000 frames ≈ 3.7 GiB) fails at construction, before any pointer allocation. `Ring.init` now reuses the configuration's precomputed `slotStride`/`sampleCapacity` instead of recomputing unchecked. New tests: `testConfigurationRejectsValuesThatDoNotFitInt32`, `testConfigurationRejectsMultiplicationOverflow` (both an Int32-range-squared byte-size overflow and a `slotCount: Int.max` overflow), `testConfigurationRejectsAbsurdButNonOverflowingAllocationSize`, `testConfigurationAcceptsExactAllocationSizeBoundaryAndRejectsOneFrameOver` (exact-boundary accept + boundary+1 reject).
    3. **Additional defensive corrections:** non-finite/non-positive `sampleRate` is now rejected via a new `.rejectedInvalidSampleRate` outcome (`testNonFiniteOrNonPositiveSampleRateIsRejected`, 5 cases: 0, -1, NaN, +inf, -inf) rather than being published as metadata; the cross-queue `consumer` closure is now `@escaping @Sendable`, and `TimecodeRealtimeIngressSlotView` is explicitly `Sendable`; `TimecodeRealtimeIngressWorker.init` is now failable (`init?`), rejecting non-finite/non-positive `drainInterval` rather than silently accepting it (`testWorkerInitRejectsNonFiniteOrNonPositiveDrainIntervals`, 5 cases: 0, -0.001, NaN, +inf, -inf) — every pre-existing successful-construction call site updated to force-unwrap.
    4. **Realtime guarantees preserved:** `publish` remains allocation-free/lock-free/dispatch-free/logging-free, with no `AVAudioPCMBuffer`/adapter/heartbeat/fixture calls — the new `sampleRate` guard is a plain comparison, no new allocation or call.
  - **Incidental finding during TSan verification:** an initial ThreadSanitizer run caught a genuine data race, but in the *test harness*, not production code — the new self-stop test's `WorkerHolder` box was `@unchecked Sendable` without real synchronization (main thread wrote `.worker` while the timer's first tick could already be reading it). Fixed by making `WorkerHolder`'s `worker` property a properly `NSLock`-protected computed property. Re-ran TSan twice after the fix: zero warnings.

Added `safeSampleTime(_ value: Float64) -> Int64?` (allocation-free, non-throwing, mirrors the existing `safeFrameCount(_:)` pattern), used in the callback as `safeSampleTime(...) ?? 0`. Audited `hostTime`/`frameCount` too — both are safe (`UInt64`→`UInt64` direct read; `UInt32`→`Int` never traps). 4 new deterministic test functions (ordinary values, NaN/±infinity, exact ±2^63 boundaries — accounting for float64 ULP at that magnitude — and astronomically large finite values).

Extracted the smallest production-used seam: `LowLatencySinkLifecycleActions` (7 closures: `attachAndConnect`, `startEngine`, `stopEngine`, `disconnectAndDetach`, `resetEngine`, `markHeartbeatInstalled`, `resetHeartbeat`) and `LowLatencySinkLifecycle` (owns `currentSession`/`isSinkAttached`/`isHeartbeatInstalled`, drives `start`/`stop` through those closures in the required order). `refreshLowLatencyTimecodeInput()`/`stopLowLatencyTimecodeInput()` were rewritten as thin wrappers building real closures around `timecodeInputEngine`/the heartbeat and calling `lowLatencySinkLifecycle.start`/`.stop` — the exact same sequencing code now runs in production and in the new `LowLatencySinkLifecycleTests` (6 tests: successful start transitions to running; failed start invokes complete cleanup with no attached sink/accepting ring/installed heartbeat/published session and the exact teardown-closure order; session-construction failure calls zero graph actions; teardown order proven via ring-state/session-state checks interleaved with the closures plus a no-further-delivery check; repeated stop is idempotent and skips a redundant detach; a stale prior session's ring stays confined/rejecting across a full start→stop→start cycle).     4. **Newly found during this pass's own verification (not one of the 3 assigned findings, fixed anyway):** the first `teardownActions()` implementation captured `[weak self]` in every closure; since `stopLowLatencyTimecodeInput()` (which calls `teardownActions()`) runs from `deinit`, forming a *new* weak reference while `self` is being deallocated triggered `objc[...]: Cannot form weak reference ... in the process of deallocation` on essentially every `MacCaptureEngine` teardown in the test suite (53 occurrences in one `scripts/build.sh all` run). Fixed by capturing `self` directly (no `[weak self]`) in all 7 lifecycle-action closures — every one of them is invoked synchronously, within the same call that constructs it, and never escapes, so there is no retain cycle and no need for a weak capture; this is exactly equivalent to the plain `self.method()` calls `deinit` already makes elsewhere in this class. Result: `route=AVCapture fallback installed=true callbacks=0 observed=awaiting callback,0ch,0f@0Hz rejection=failed to synthesize AVAudioPCMBuffer for delivered slot`; `DVS sink ring: published=23246 dropped=0 rejected=0` (ring/worker themselves healthy — tens of thousands of real render-quantum slots were actually published); `DVS pipeline ingress: received=17911...` (the separate, pre-existing legacy-path diagnostic, confirming ordinary AVCapture fallback kept working correctly throughout, as required).
  - **Root cause, reproduced deterministically (not guessed) via a standalone script before touching any code:** `handleLowLatencySinkDelivery` reconstructed a *generic* `AVAudioFormat(commonFormat:sampleRate:channels:interleaved:)` from only sample rate + channel count. For 14 channels this returns `nil` — there is no standard channel layout for that count — so the very first optional in the guard chain failed, before `AVAudioPCMBuffer` allocation was ever reached (confirmed separately: given a format that *does* carry an explicit channel layout, `AVAudioPCMBuffer` construction succeeds immediately). Every real Rane callback hit this and was silently rejected; heartbeat `callbackCount` stayed 0 forever, so `isFresh` never became true, so `processAudioSampleBuffer` never suppressed the legacy path — the app just kept running invisibly on AVCapture fallback the whole time.
  - **Fix:** `refreshLowLatencyTimecodeInput()` now captures the exact, already-validated `tapFormat` (the same format used to `connect` the input node to the sink node — a real `AVAudioIONode` format, which — like all real hardware formats — always carries a proper channel layout) directly into the worker's `consumer` closure, and passes it straight through to a new pure `makeLowLatencySinkPCMBuffer(fromSlot:format:)` — no reconstruction, ever. This eliminated the failure mode by construction; no fallback "planar-data-direct" adapter entry point was needed (the preferred fix alone was sufficient, confirmed by a new regression test using a real 14-channel discrete-layout format at 512 frames/48kHz). Also added: `LowLatencySinkAdapterConversionFailureStage` (identifies exactly which stage failed — `channelCountMismatch`/`bufferAllocation`/`channelDataUnavailable` — included in the rejection message alongside actual channel/frame/sample-rate metadata, replacing the old generic "failed to synthesize..." string); `LowLatencySinkWorkerObservationDiagnostic` (a new, separate `DVS sink observed:` diagnostic line recording the worker's raw per-slot frame/channel/sample-rate evidence on *every* delivery, success or failure — so real hardware evidence is visible even while adapter conversion is failing, without ever touching or substituting for the heartbeat's own freshness/fallback-suppression state, which still only updates on full adapter success).

Actual `AVAudioEngine` tap callbacks remain exactly 4,800 frames at 48 kHz (100 ms) — ≠ the 256-frame tap request, ≠ the HAL's 512-frame buffer size, ≠ the HAL's own advertised maximum of 4,096 frames. The 100 ms batching therefore occurs in the software path after the HAL device boundary, not in the Rane's native I/O quantum.
  - **Decisive root cause (proven from the installed SDK header `AVAudioNode.h`):** `installTapOnBus:bufferSize:format:block:` documents `bufferSize` as supporting a range of **[100, 400] ms**, not arbitrary frame counts. ScratchLab's 256-frame (5.3 ms) request is outside that range; the observed 4,800-frame callbacks are exactly the documented 100 ms floor. The Rane hardware is already running at its own native 10.7 ms (512-frame) quantum — `AVAudioNode` tap aggregation, not the hardware, creates the 100 ms latency floor. **Do not fix this by writing the shared HAL buffer property, `MaximumFramesPerSlice`, or another `installTap` buffer size** — none of those addresses the actual cause.

- [x] Reconcile the dirty `TimecodeCMSampleBufferAdapter.swift` against the committed `9f54937` retention mechanism (uncommitted)
  - Files: `ScratchLab/Models/TimecodeCMSampleBufferAdapter.swift` (production fix), `ScratchLabDesktopTests/DVSControlVinylAnalyzerTests.swift` (new/strengthened tests). Not staged, committed, or pushed — both remain dirty.
  - Completed: implemented the minimal fix identified by the prior read-only audit. Root cause — the generation-based transaction retention mechanism from `9f54937` was intact and unweakened in the dirty tree, but the dirty tree had also wired a second real production producer (the low-latency `AVAudioEngine` tap, `stereoSampleResult(fromPCMBuffer:hostTime:)`, called from `MacCaptureEngine.swift` on a different thread than the legacy `AVCaptureAudioDataOutput` path) into the same shared retention state. The generation counter only bumped on reset/selection-change, never on a successful commit, so two concurrent transactions captured at the same generation could both commit, the second silently overwriting the first (a lost update) — a scenario the mechanism was never proven safe against. Fix: `commitRetentionTransaction` now bumps `storedRetentionGeneration` on every successful commit too, reusing the existing CAS-style guard — no new lock, no API change, no change to hysteresis/lock boundaries/automatic-vs-explicit pair-selection semantics. Whichever of two overlapping transactions commits first wins; the other's now-stale snapshot is rejected exactly like a post-reset stale commit, its own selected audio still returned normally, only its retention bookkeeping dropped. Corrected the doc comments on `storedRetentionGeneration`/`RetentionTransactionSnapshot`, which previously described this as an unresolved concern and pointed to a nonexistent "reconciliation audit note."
  - Tests: added `testConcurrentTransactionsFromTwoProducersRejectsTheStaleCommit` (deterministic, sequential — no threads, no timing dependency — the direct regression for the exact race); strengthened `testConcurrentSelectionAndResetVersusRetentionUpdatesLeavesConsistentFinalState` with a second independent concurrent producer queue simulating the low-latency path; corrected the misleading intro comment above this test group that incorrectly claimed a prior audit found the production mechanism "stripped out" (it hadn't — the real gap was the unproven second producer).

- [x] Pipeline output synchronization — make `TimecodeControlPipeline`'s decoded output state race-safe and transactionally coherent
  - **Revised after three rounds of review correction; final completed scope covers only the ten decoded outputs.** The first pass (whole-body `outputLock` on all four transactional methods) was found to reintroduce the exact SwiftUI stall risk (0.4–2.1s) the pipeline had previously been measured to have, and still didn't give `makeValidationSnapshot` an atomic multi-property read — replaced by the minimal-critical-section design below (round 1). A round-2 attempt to also lock `mode`'s `didSet` was later found insufficient and reverted in round 3 (see that section below): `mode`/configuration synchronization is **not** part of this task's completed scope and is tracked separately by the immediately following unchecked task. Final accepted state is `TimecodeLiveTapTests` **46/46** (not round 2's 47/47 — that count included the since-reverted `testModeTransitionSerializesWithInFlightFlush`). Nothing from any pass was ever staged or committed.
  - Files: `ScratchLab/Models/TimecodeControlPipeline.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/TimecodeLiveTapTests.swift`. `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`, `AI_HANDOFF.md`, `AI_HANDOFF/next_prompt.md` updated after verification.
  - Done when: the ten decoded outputs (`latestPlatterTimeline`, `latestDecodeResult`, `latestDiagnosis`, `latestClassification`, `counters`, `currentDirection`, `currentRate`, `lastDropReason`, `signalHealth`, `lastBufferReceivedAt`) are published as one atomic snapshot, never observable mid-transaction; getters never block through a decode/diagnostics/filter/adapt pass; `makeValidationSnapshot` derives every field from one captured snapshot, not per-property reads (while separate, independent getter calls may legitimately straddle two different complete generations — documented, not a bug); each complete mutation transaction in `pushStereoBuffer`/`flushDecode`/`reset`/`resetCounters` is serialized against other writers; the coalesced one-invalidation-per-changed-transaction `objectWillChange` behavior is preserved and never delivered while `outputLock` or `processingLock` is held; the eight `@Published` configuration properties are untouched; ingestion-lock behavior and lock ordering are preserved/documented; no decoder constants, sign mappings, `invertDirection`, frequency-ratio behavior, validation rules, or output values change; Thread Sanitizer is clean. **`mode`/configuration synchronization is explicitly excluded from this Done-when.** The round-2 attempt to fold it into `mode`'s `didSet` was found insufficient and reverted (see the third review correction below) — it is tracked as its own scoping/design decision by the immediately following unchecked task, not satisfied by this one.
  - Root cause confirmed before editing (unchanged from the original pass): the ten outputs were plain unsynchronized stored properties, mutated by `pushStereoBuffer`/`flushDecode`/`reset`/`resetCounters` from `DVSControlProcessingLoop`'s dedicated serial `com.scratchlab.dvsControl` background queue while SwiftUI view bodies (`TimecodeControlCard`, `DVSControlVinylPanel`) and DEBUG main-thread self-test buttons touch the same properties on the main thread. The pre-existing `lock` (`NSLock`) only ever protected the ingestion accumulator/queue, never this output state.
  - Completed (revised design): a private `OutputState` struct bundles all ten outputs as one `Equatable` value. `outputLock` (`NSLock`) guards only the single published `_publishedOutput: OutputState` — a trivial copy on read, trivial swap-in on publish, never held across decode/diagnostics/filter/adapt/recorder work or `objectWillChange` delivery. A new `processingLock` (`NSLock`) serializes the four writer methods and the pipeline-internal state a transaction mutates alongside its output (`stabilityFilterState`, `cumulativePlatterPosition`, `pendingDiagnosticsBufferCount`, `lastDiagnosticsPublishAudioTime`, `decodeSessionStartDate`, the diagnostics tap/engine). Each transaction reads the current snapshot into a local `var state: OutputState` under `processingLock`, mutates only that local copy for its whole body (`runDiagnostics`/`publishPendingDiagnosticsBufferCount` now take `inout OutputState`), and calls `publish(state)` — **still holding `processingLock`**, released only afterward, so a second transaction can never start from a stale snapshot and clobber the first's published update (a lost-update ordering bug caught and fixed during this same revision, before any test run, by publishing before releasing `processingLock` rather than after). The ten public getters became plain computed properties reading the published snapshot. `makeValidationSnapshot` captures the snapshot once and derives every field (including a locally-shadowed `counters`) from that single value. `reset()` publishes one fresh default `OutputState()`; `resetCounters()` mutates only `counters`/`lastDropReason` on a locally-read copy. A `#if DEBUG`-only `debugPrePublishHook: (() -> Void)?` was added inside `publish(_:)`, invoked once per transaction immediately before the swap — always `nil` outside tests, used to pause a transaction deterministically for the new tests below. Documented lock ordering: `processingLock` is always acquired before the pre-existing ingestion `lock` when a transaction needs both; `outputLock` is independent of both and never acquires either from within its own critical section, so it cannot deadlock against them; `enqueueLiveStereoBuffer`/`liveIngressDiagnostics` only ever take `lock` alone, unchanged. Eight `@Published` configuration properties, all decoder/pipeline calibration math, and all output values are unchanged — confirmed via full regression re-run.
  - Tests replaced (in `ScratchLabDesktopTests/TimecodeLiveTapTests.swift`; 44 → 46 tests): removed 2 remaining probabilistic busy-loop tests (`testResetProducesCoherentDefaultStateEvenWithConcurrentReaders`, `testResetCountersProducesCoherentCounterStateWithoutTouchingOtherOutputs`) that raced a busy-loop reader against a writer and hoped to catch a violation within a bounded iteration count — absence of a violation there proves nothing if the window was simply never hit. Added 5 deterministic tests built on `debugPrePublishHook`, which pauses a transaction after all processing has finished but before publish, giving the test exact control over the interleaving: `testReaderDuringInFlightFlushObservesOnlyPreOrPostTransactionSnapshot` (readers see only the complete pre- or post-flush snapshot, never a partial one), `testOutputGettersReturnImmediatelyWhileTransactionIsPaused` (getters return in well under 0.5s even while paused — the direct regression check for the UI-stall bug the coarse design would have reintroduced), `testMakeValidationSnapshotNeverMixesTransactionGenerations` (every field of a repeatedly-sampled snapshot agrees with one generation as a whole, never a mix of two), `testResetPublishesOneCompleteStateNotPartial`, `testResetCountersPublishesCountersAndDropReasonAtomically` (both prove `reset`/`resetCounters` publish their whole result atomically, not field-by-field). Kept `testConcurrentDebugMainThreadAndDVSWorkerMutationSerializeSafely` (deterministic regardless of scheduling — `processingLock` serialization guarantees the exact expected `totalBuffersReceived` sum on every run, not a race to catch) and the two pre-existing coalesced-publication tests (`testRepeatedIdenticalEmptyFlushDoesNotRepublishCounters`, `testDecodeFlushPublishesOneCoherentOutputChange`). Removed the stale doc comment describing the old per-property-lock "mirrored-field agreement" invariant, which no longer applies to the atomic-snapshot design.
  - Self-correction during this revision: while writing the four-method defer pattern, the first draft released `processingLock` *before* calling `publish(state)`. That would have let a second writer thread acquire `processingLock`, read a stale (not-yet-published) snapshot, and publish over the first transaction's result — a lost-update race. Caught by re-reasoning through the design before running any test, not by a failing test; fixed by publishing first and releasing `processingLock` afterward in all four methods.

    1. `objectWillChange` could still be delivered while `processingLock` was held: the (already-fixed-once) `publish(_:)` did the atomic swap *and* the notification before returning, and callers called it before `processingLock.unlock()`. Split `publish(_:)` into `swapPublishedOutput(_:) -> Bool` (swap-only, under `outputLock`, called while `processingLock` is still held — preserving the lost-update fix above) and left `sendChangeNotification()` to be called by each of the four methods' `defer` only *after* `processingLock.unlock()`. Neither lock is now ever held while `objectWillChange` is sent.
    2. `mode`'s `didSet` mutated `accumulatedStereoInputs` (ingestion-`lock`-protected) and `cumulativePlatterPosition` (`processingLock`-protected) as a direct, unguarded mutation when leaving `.controlPrototype` — racing a concurrent `flushDecode()`/`reset()`. Fixed by wrapping that clear in `processingLock` → `lock`, the same order the four transactional methods already use.
    3. Doc comments overstated cross-getter coherence: the atomic swap makes any *one* getter call (or `makeValidationSnapshot`, which captures one snapshot for every field) generation-coherent, but two *separate* getter calls made back-to-back are two independent snapshot reads and may legitimately straddle two different, each internally complete, generations if a transaction publishes between them — expected, not a bug. Stated explicitly now above the getters and in `makeValidationSnapshot`'s doc comment.

- [x] Close the `mode`/configuration cross-thread race in `TimecodeControlPipeline`
  - Files: `ScratchLab/Models/TimecodeControlPipeline.swift`, `ScratchLab/Models/TimecodePrototypeProfile.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/TimecodeLiveTapTests.swift`. `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`, `AI_HANDOFF.md`, `AI_HANDOFF/next_prompt.md` updated after verification.
  - Background: found and reverted, not fixed, during the pipeline output synchronization task above (see its "Third review correction" note). `mode` and the other seven configuration properties (`liveTapEnabled`, `invertDirection`, `rateScale`, `inputChannel`, `minConfidence`, `maxRate`, `signalThresholdRMS`) were plain `@Published` stored properties. Swift writes a `@Published` property's backing storage before its `didSet` runs, so no amount of locking inside a `didSet` body could make the property's own read/write access itself race-safe — the four transactional methods (`pushStereoBuffer`, `flushDecode`, `reset`, `resetCounters`) each read one or more of these configuration properties, some while holding `processingLock` and some not, while `mode`/etc. were written from the main thread (SwiftUI bindings, DEBUG self-test buttons, preset application) with no synchronization at all.
  - Completed: a private `ConfigurationState` struct bundles all eight properties as one `Equatable` value, mirroring the existing `OutputState`/`outputLock` design for decoded outputs. A new `configurationLock` (`NSLock`) guards only the single published `_publishedConfiguration` — a trivial copy on read, trivial swap-in on write. All eight properties became computed `get`/`set` pairs over that snapshot (no more `@Published`); every setter goes through one shared `updateConfiguration(_:)` helper that reads the current snapshot, applies a mutation closure, compares, swaps, and — only after `configurationLock` is released — sends `objectWillChange`, but only if the value actually changed (an assignment identical to the current value never notifies). `mode`'s setter additionally performs the leaving-`.controlPrototype` cleanup (clearing `accumulatedStereoInputs`/`cumulativePlatterPosition`) *after* the configuration write, under the documented `processingLock` → `lock` ordering the four transactional methods already use — `configurationLock` is always released before `processingLock` is acquired for this cleanup, never nested, so the two locks can never deadlock against each other. `pushStereoBuffer`, `flushDecode`, `enqueueLiveStereoBuffer`, and `makeValidationSnapshot` each now capture a `ConfigurationState` snapshot exactly once at their boundary (`captureConfigurationForOperation()`) and use only that captured value for their entire body — `decoder`/`stabilityFilter` (previously computed properties reading live `signalThresholdRMS`/`minConfidence`) became functions taking those values as explicit parameters, and `applyChannelSelection`/`accumulateStereoInput`/`runDiagnostics` take an explicit `channel`/`mode` parameter instead of reading the live property. Confirmed no `$property` projected-publisher usage of any of the eight properties exists anywhere in the repo before removing `@Published` (only SwiftUI's `$pipeline.<property>` dynamic-member bindings, which work identically over any settable property regardless of `@Published`). Added a public `applyCalibrationBatch(inputChannel:invertDirection:rateScale:minConfidence:maxRate:)` that sets all five profile-controlled fields as one `updateConfiguration` call; `TimecodePrototypeProfile.apply(to:)` now calls it instead of five sequential property writes, finally making its pre-existing "atomically" doc comment true. No decoder constants, sign mappings, calibration math, thresholds, output-state synchronization, capture, playback, notation, export, or UI behavior changed — only how these eight values are stored and threaded through.
  - Tests added (`ScratchLabDesktopTests/TimecodeLiveTapTests.swift`, 46 → 52): `testFlushPausedAfterConfigurationCaptureUsesOldGenerationThenNextFlushUsesNewGeneration` (a paused transaction keeps using its captured old generation throughout; the next transaction observes the new one), `testProfileApplicationIsAtomicNotObservablePartiallyApplied` (a transaction paused mid-flight sees one profile's `minConfidence` and `invertDirection` together, never a cross-mix with a concurrently-applied second profile), `testModeTransitionCleanupSerializesWithInFlightFlush` (the `mode` value itself changes immediately and independently, but the accumulator/position cleanup still serializes behind an in-flight flush's `processingLock`), `testConfigurationGettersReturnImmediatelyWhileTransactionIsPaused` (all eight getters return in well under 0.5s even while a transaction is paused holding `processingLock`), `testUnchangedConfigurationAssignmentDoesNotNotify`, `testChangedIndividualAndBatchConfigurationUpdatesNotifyExactlyOnce` (a changed batch update notifies exactly once, not once per field; an unchanged batch does not notify). All six use the new DEBUG-only `debugPostConfigurationCaptureHook` (fires immediately after `captureConfigurationForOperation()`, mirroring `debugPrePublishHook`'s existing pattern for `OutputState`) or the existing `debugPrePublishHook` to deterministically pause and control the exact interleaving, not a probabilistic busy-loop.

  - **Linearization correction (same day, applied after the above):** a further review found the design above, while genuinely race-safe (no torn reads, no lost updates, no memory race under TSan), still allowed two *ordering* bugs, because being individually atomic is not the same as being linearized against a related operation. **Scenario 1:** `pushStereoBuffer`/`flushDecode` captured their `ConfigurationState` snapshot *before* acquiring `processingLock`. An operation that captured mode while still `.controlPrototype`, then got delayed, and only resumed *after* a concurrent transition to `.disabled` had already published the new mode and cleared the accumulator, would still append/decode using its stale captured mode — leaking audio into a session that was supposed to have been cleared. **Scenario 2:** `mode`'s setter published its new configuration value *before* waiting for `processingLock` — only the accumulator/position cleanup that followed was serialized by it. A transition that published `.disabled`, then got delayed before its cleanup ran, while a second transition (e.g. an immediate re-enable) completed in between, would have its delayed cleanup run *after* the second transition and incorrectly wipe the new session's already-accumulated data.
  - Fix: `pushStereoBuffer` and `flushDecode` now call `captureConfigurationForOperation()` only *after* `processingLock.lock()` — the whole operation, including any ingestion-`lock`-guarded mutation, is now linearized against `mode` transitions. `mode`'s setter was rewritten to acquire `processingLock` *first*, before writing anything — the entire write-plus-cleanup is now one `processingLock` critical section, so two mode transitions can never interleave and a transactional operation either completes entirely before a transition starts or entirely after it finishes cleaning up. Leaving `.controlPrototype` now also clears the live-ingress pending queue/bookkeeping (`pendingLiveStereoInputs`, `pendingLiveFrameCount`, `pendingLiveReceivedCount`, not just the direct-push accumulator as before), while preserving lifetime diagnostic totals (`totalLiveReceivedCount`, `totalLiveDroppedForLatencyCount`, `latestLiveBufferReceivedAt`) — only `reset()` clears those. `enqueueLiveStereoBuffer` — which must remain callback-safe and never touch `processingLock` (a real-time audio callback must not block behind DSP work) — now holds `configurationLock` continuously from its mode check through its ingestion-`lock`-guarded append, so it linearizes against a mode transition's cleanup of the same pending-ingress state via `configurationLock` alone: whichever of the two acquires `configurationLock` first completes entirely before the other can proceed. Documented the full global lock order: `processingLock` → `configurationLock` → the ingestion `lock` for anything touching more than one (mode's setter, `pushStereoBuffer`, `flushDecode`); `enqueueLiveStereoBuffer` uses only `configurationLock` → `lock`, never `processingLock`; `outputLock` remains independent of all three. `mode` now correctly reads its *old* value for the whole duration a transition is blocked behind an in-flight transaction (previously it changed immediately, independent of `processingLock`) — a deliberate correctness trade against the same bounded, occasional writer-vs-writer contention already accepted for the other three transactional methods.
  - Tests added/updated (`ScratchLabDesktopTests/TimecodeLiveTapTests.swift`, 52 → 56): renamed/rewrote `testModeTransitionCleanupSerializesWithInFlightFlush` → `testModeTransitionIsFullySerializedBehindInFlightFlushNotJustItsCleanup` (corrected its assertion — `mode` must read the *old* value while blocked, not the new one, matching the corrected design). Added `testPausedPushStereoBufferWithOldControlConfigCannotAppendAfterDisableCleanup` and `testPausedEnqueueLiveStereoBufferWithOldControlConfigCannotAppendAfterDisableCleanup` (direct regressions for Scenario 1 on both ingestion paths — pause via `debugPostConfigurationCaptureHook`, which now also fires inside `mode`'s setter after its configuration write; prove a concurrent disable blocks until the paused operation completes, then that a flush after re-enable decodes nothing), `testDisableImmediatelyFollowedByReenableDoesNotClearTheNewSession` (Scenario 2 — pause disable between its write and cleanup, prove a concurrent re-enable cannot even publish until disable's whole transition finishes, then that the new session decodes normally), and `testConcurrentModeTransitionsPushEnqueueAndFlushDoNotDeadlock` (200-iteration, 4-thread stress test exercising every new lock combination). One test-authoring pitfall caught and fixed during this pass: the two new "paused with old control config" tests initially deadlocked, because `mode`'s setter now *also* fires `debugPostConfigurationCaptureHook`, and the tests hadn't cleared the hook before starting their concurrent mode-transition thread — that thread's own call into the (still-assigned) push/enqueue pause closure blocked forever on an already-consumed semaphore. Fixed by clearing the hook immediately after confirming the initial pause, before starting the mode-transition thread, in both tests.

  - Completed: Rane physical pair 3/4 maps physical channel 3 → left, physical channel 4 → right (confirmed identically in both the live pair-selection path and the fixture recorder in `TimecodeCMSampleBufferAdapter.swift`). The decoder's raw, uncalibrated direction (`TimecodePhaseDecoder.extractPhaseDelta`'s `phaseDelta = rightPhase − leftPhase`, `< −30° → forward`/`> +30° → backward`) is inverted relative to true physical platter direction on every single window measured — raw output says "backward" during confirmed-forward motion and "forward" during confirmed-backward motion, 115/115. `TimecodePrototypeProfile.raneOneMkiiDebug().invertDirection = true`, applied in `TimecodeControlPipeline`'s calibration (`sign = invertDirection ? −1 : 1`, flips velocity and `direction.inverted`), correctly compensates this inversion in every case. Final calibrated pipeline direction and velocity sign matched the fixture catalog's ground truth 115/115, zero exceptions. Separately, `TimecodeSignalDiagnostics.estimatePhaseOffset` (a cross-correlation-lag phase estimate, used only for UI display and the sign-insensitive `isQuadrature` classifier — never consumed by the decoder or pipeline) uses the mathematically opposite sign convention from the decoder's atan2-subtraction (proven both symbolically via a discrete spike-timing derivation and empirically: diagnostics ≈ −decoder-delta to within one cross-correlation quantization step on every window), and its own doc comment ("Positive = R leads L") is itself backwards relative to what its own code computes. Neither inconsistency has any functional/behavioral effect on production direction or velocity.
  - Conclusion: **NO CHANGE REQUIRED.** The decoder's raw sign rule, `TimecodeControlPipeline`'s calibration formula, and `invertDirection = true` in the Rane profile are correct and mutually consistent, confirmed against real hardware across all speeds and both directions with zero exceptions — this is the stable, working contract, not a bug to fix. Decoder sign rules, pipeline calibration, and the Rane profile were not changed. - [x] Fix the dense estimator's fail-closed gap violation and chunking-dependent envelope
  - Files: `ScratchLab/Models/TimecodeSignedRateDenseEstimator.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/TimecodeSignedRateDenseEstimatorTests.swift`, workflow documentation
  - Done when: the gap path resets envelope and crossing continuity together so no post-gap classification depends on pre-gap amplitude; the envelope is a per-real-sample streaming power tracker (no per-call/per-block blend constant) so it is chunking-invariant under variable amplitude, not just constant amplitude; the no-signal marker count/timestamp's remaining block-shaped behavior is precisely documented and tested rather than left as an unstated exception; deterministic tests that fail on the pre-fix implementation cover variable-amplitude chunking invariance, gap-then-silence honest no-signal, and gap-then-weak-signal non-fabrication; the full 15-fixture offline audit is rerun at the documented threshold/gap configurations since the envelope fix changes real measurements; DVS-adjacent regressions, capture-pipeline fixtures, `scripts/build.sh all`, `plutil -lint`, and `git diff --check` are run; no other production file changes; nothing is committed.
  - Completed: found two real bugs in the just-landed dense estimator (task below). (1) The gap branch called `resetContinuity()` only, leaving `envelopeLeft`/`envelopeRight`/`hasEnvelope` untouched — a loud pre-gap envelope survived across a detected gap and could bias post-gap silence/ambiguity gating, contradicting the type's own documented fail-closed contract. (2) The envelope was a single block-aggregate-RMS blend applied once per `consume` call using that call's own block duration; for a variable-amplitude signal this produced different envelope histories depending purely on how the caller chunked identical audio — every existing test used constant amplitude and never exposed it. Fixed by replacing the block-level blend with a per-real-sample exponential power tracker (`power += alpha · (sample² − power)`, `alpha` from the real per-sample elapsed time `1/sampleRate` and `envelopeTimeConstant` — identical regardless of chunking) and adding a `resetContinuityAndEnvelope()` used on the gap path. The once-per-call silence gate remains intentionally block-shaped; this is now stated precisely in the type's doc comment and pinned by a dedicated test rather than left implicit. Added and verified 4 new tests that each independently fail against the pre-fix code and pass against the fix: variable-amplitude one-block/irregular-block/per-sample-block equality (including final estimator state, not just output samples); gap-after-loud-motion-then-short-silence honest no-signal; gap-after-loud-motion-then-weak-signal (no stale-envelope inheritance, no fabricated `.available` direction); and the narrowed no-signal-marker-shape guarantee. Re-ran the full 15-fixture offline audit at all three previously-documented threshold/gap configurations (no new tuning): at default `0.05`/`0.25s`, `start_stop`/`needle_lift` remain at zero false reversals; `fine_flicks` genuine commits are now 10 (was 5) against the established 84 baseline, `baby_scratch` 20 (was 22) against 307; at `0.02`, `start_stop`=1/`needle_lift`=2 false with `fine_flicks`=36/`baby_scratch`=105 genuine; at `0.01`, `start_stop`=5/`needle_lift`=2 false with `fine_flicks`=64/`baby_scratch`=231 genuine; `slow_reversals`/`fast_reversals` unaffected. The trade-off conclusion is unchanged — no threshold cleanly separates false near-stop evidence from genuine fast/fine content; only the precise counts shifted a few percent now that the envelope math is correct. Also corrected a documentation self-contradiction (both claiming `git add -N`/`git reset` was used and that nothing was reset) in `AI_HANDOFF.md`/`DEV_LOG.md`/this file, and reconciled a `fine_flicks` baseline denominator in this file that had been mis-copied as `230` instead of `84`.   - Files: `ScratchLabDesktop/Services/ScratchSamplePlaybackController.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/ScratchSamplePlaybackControllerTests.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/DVSOfflineGrainRendererTests.swift`, workflow documentation

  - Completed: `ScratchSamplePlaybackController` no longer applies `AVAudioPlayerNode.interrupts` (or the artificial zero-edge fade) to DVS-driven direction changes — only the legacy MIDI reversal path retains that behavior. DVS reversals now preserve the existing bounded (~4 ms) queue reserve and enqueue the reversed grain after it, so direction-change latency is bounded by that reserve instead of an audible truncation. `testDVSReversalPreservesTheBoundedQueueCushion` pins the output-window/reserve math across a reversal. The offline renderer's three saved-fixture tests were extended with `report.overlapCount == 0` assertions for `slow_reversals`/`fast_reversals`, both now green (previously 19/45 interrupted overlaps). Removing the interrupt path surfaced more measured "gaps" than before (`slow_reversals`: 36 gaps/3.45s; `fast_reversals`: 43 gaps/1.21s) because the interrupts branch used to bypass gap detection entirely (an interrupt never leaves silence — it truncates instead) rather than genuinely eliminate the underlying no-drive intervals. - [x] Diagnose synthetic quadrature live decode instability and CPU load
  - Files: `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`, `ScratchLab/Models/TimecodePhaseDecoder.swift`, `ScratchLab/Models/TimecodeControlPipeline.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktop/Views/MacAnalyzerView.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/TimecodeDecoderTests.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/TimecodeLiveTapTests.swift`
  - Done when: deterministic direct decoder/pipeline tests feed continuous 1 kHz quadrature buffers and prove whether the instability is in direct decode or live capture, constant quadrature produces confidence above threshold with accepted samples and near-zero rate, live tap CPU pressure is reduced without decoder threshold tuning, audio callback work stays minimal, no decoder/RANE/replay/platter/notation/export/schema compatibility claims are changed, focused timecode tests pass, and the required build result is recorded
  - Completed: root cause was in the prototype decoder and live publishing path, not Serato/FSK. Direct-feed regression reproduced the synthetic quadrature instability before the fix, which showed the bug was not only Loopback/AVCapture. The decoder used per-buffer quadrature correlation that assumes integer carrier cycles; 1024-frame live buffers at 44.1 kHz / 1 kHz are non-integer windows, producing deterministic phase wobble. The pipeline also timestamped buffers from wall-clock `Date()`, inflating rate under callback/UI jitter, and the live tap posted one main-queue block plus multiple published diagnostics updates for every audio callback. Fixed by using continuous audio-frame timestamps, least-squares phase fitting over the sine/cosine basis, a small decoder numerical stationary deadband for residual phase velocity, 10 Hz diagnostics publishing while preserving every decode buffer, and callback coalescing before the main queue. Focused verification passed: `xcodebuild -project ScratchLab.xcodeproj -scheme ScratchLabDesktop -destination 'platform=macOS' -only-testing:ScratchLabDesktopTests/TimecodeDecoderTests/testTimecodeDecoderConstantQuadratureProducesNearZeroRate -only-testing:ScratchLabDesktopTests/TimecodeLiveTapTests -only-testing:ScratchLabDesktopTests/TimecodeLiveIntegrationTests/testCountersTrackAcceptedAndDropped test` (`20/20`). `git diff --check` passed. `./scripts/build.sh` passed the Python capture fixtures (`47/47`) but failed in pre-existing broad desktop/practice/notation/resource tests; the final failure list did not include timecode tests.

- [x] Generate local-only pure quadrature Timecode Prototype test WAV
  - Files: `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`, `scripts/generate_quadrature_test_wav.py`
