  - Completed: root cause was that Timecode Prototype live-path validation needed a synthetic decoder-control signal without using Serato Control CD or FSK material. Added a standard-library Python generator that writes the WAV into ignored `debug_captures/timecode/`, prints the sample rate, duration, frequency, amplitude, phase relationship, and Loopback validation route, and leaves the generated WAV as a local artifact only. Verified the file header and samples: stereo, 44,100 Hz, 16-bit PCM, 2,646,000 frames, exactly 60.000000 s, peak left/right `0.419996`, RMS balanced at about `0.296986`, and quarter-shift correlation `0.999994`. `python3 -m py_compile scripts/generate_quadrature_test_wav.py` passed. `./scripts/build.sh` passed the capture-pipeline fixture tests (`47/47`) but failed in pre-existing/unrelated app tests during the `ScratchLabDesktop` XCTest leg; no decoder, threshold, RANE/replay/platter, notation, export, schema, app bundle resource, signing, or entitlement files were changed.

- [x] Fix VirtualPlatter audio control so record movement owns sample playback
  - Files: `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`, `ScratchLab/Models/VirtualPlatter.swift`, `ScratchLab/Views/VirtualPlatterPrototypeView.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/VirtualPlatterTests.swift`
  - Done when: the clean `ahhh` sample is no longer fired as a detached one-shot, the fixed cue remains in the middle of the gray drill area, record phase maps directly to sample position, holding the record stops/holds audio immediately, forward motion advances through the sample, backward motion reads backward through the sample, capture/export/ML/dataset code is untouched, iOS builds, and VirtualPlatter tests pass
  - Completed: root cause was that `VirtualPlatterAudio` used `AVAudioPlayer` as a clean one-shot: `advanceRecord` triggered the full sample when the wrapped record phase crossed the cue, then the player kept running independently of later platter motion. Fixed by adding a pure `VirtualPlatterSampleMapper` for cue-to-sample-position mapping and replacing the one-shot player with an `AVAudioEngine`/`AVAudioPlayerNode` short-buffer reader. Each tick now derives the sample frame from `recordPhase`; zero movement or prep-zone phase calls `hold()` and stops scheduling; forward movement schedules a short forward PCM chunk; backward movement schedules a reversed PCM chunk from the same record-owned position. Verification: `xcodebuild -project ScratchLab.xcodeproj -scheme ScratchLabDesktop -destination 'platform=macOS' test -only-testing:ScratchLabDesktopTests/VirtualPlatterTests` passed with 20 tests; `xcodebuild -project ScratchLab.xcodeproj -scheme ScratchLab -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' build` passed; `./scripts/build.sh` passed.

- [x] Surface Advanced movement/audio diagnostics and fix macOS routine audio "No signal" false-silence path
  - Files: `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`, `ScratchLab/Models/ScratchMotionAnalysis.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktop/Services/MacCaptureEngine.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktop/Views/MacAnalyzerView.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/CaptureReliabilityPhase1Tests.swift`
  - Done when: Advanced → Performance Diagnostics visibly shows dedicated `Movement Diagnostics` and `Audio Diagnostics` sections with the requested counters/reasons, movement diagnostics are no longer compiled out of non-DEBUG desktop builds, routine audio diagnostics expose selected device / attached input / buffer counts / signal reason / event counts / recent peak+rms, lower-level routed virtual audio bursts no longer collapse into one long `silenceGap` purely because fixed absolute floors are too high, existing truthful movement/audio-only behavior stays intact, and the requested build/test/script/diff verification is either complete or any remaining blocker is called out explicitly
  - Completed: root cause split between transient diagnostics state and audio threshold reporting. `MacCaptureEngine` only exposed live routine diagnostics, then cleared them when capture stopped, so Review/Advanced fell back to misleading zeros even when the take itself had valid counters. Separately, the audio UI conflated “buffers are arriving” with “signal threshold was met”, which allowed nonzero peak/RMS to coexist with a misleading `Signal 0%` status and hid whether the detector was actually below threshold or truly had no buffers. Fixed by persisting `lastCompletedRoutineMovementDiagnostics` and `lastCompletedRoutineAudioDiagnostics`, letting Advanced render those snapshots after recording stops, showing `No capture diagnostics yet` when no take has been recorded, expanding Audio Diagnostics with buffer-flow/activity/threshold state, and changing the signal status text to distinguish `No input`, `No signal`, `Below signal threshold`, and real signal percentages. Existing movement sampling, forward/back normalization, truthful renderer behavior, and audio-only honesty remain intact. Verification: `xcodebuild -project ScratchLab.xcodeproj -scheme ScratchLabDesktop -destination 'platform=macOS' build` passed; `xcodebuild -project ScratchLab.xcodeproj -scheme ScratchLabDesktop -destination 'platform=macOS' test` passed with `377` XCTest cases and `35` Swift Testing cases; `python3 scripts/test_capture_pipeline.py` passed (`47/47`); `git diff --check` passed.

- [x] Fix routine capture forward/back direction inversion at the detection source of truth
  - Files: `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`, `ScratchLabDesktop/Services/HandDirectionTracker.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktop/Services/MacCaptureEngine.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/NotationSheetTests.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/CaptureReliabilityPhase1Tests.swift`
  - Done when: manual routine capture no longer reports or draws the opposite record direction, live hand-tracking direction is normalized once at the detection source instead of only swapping UI labels, saved/exported `recordMovementEvents` continue to carry truthful `forward` / `backward` strings from the fixed source mapping, notation rendering still follows those event strings directly, audio-only partial notation still stays direction-pending without fake strokes, and the requested macOS build/test plus `python3 scripts/test_capture_pipeline.py`, `./scripts/build.sh`, and `git diff --check` are run or any remaining verification blocker is called out explicitly
  - Completed: root cause was upstream of rendering: `HandDirectionTracker` reports raw unmirrored camera-space X motion, and `MacCaptureEngine` was directly treating positive-X motion as semantic record `forward` and negative-X as `backward`. Manual capture showed that assumption was inverted for the real deck/camera setup, so the wrong semantic direction propagated into live guidance, saved `recordMovementEvents`, exported notation JSON, and any captured notation preview that honestly rendered those events. Fixed by adding one shared `normalizedRecordDirection(forCameraSpaceDirection:)` helper in `MacCaptureEngine` and routing live tracker direction through it before publishing `HandMotionState`, which means forward/back detection, captured notation events, and downstream rendering all inherit the corrected source mapping. Added regressions for the normalization helper, source-level guard coverage, detected-preview forward/back preservation, renderer use of event-direction strings, audio-only honesty, and no-CXL-string behavior. Verification so far: `xcodebuild -project ScratchLab.xcodeproj -scheme ScratchLabDesktop -destination 'platform=macOS' build` passed, `python3 scripts/test_capture_pipeline.py` passed (`47/47`), `git diff --check` passed, the first direct `xcodebuild ... test` attempt was invalid because it overlapped another Xcode job and hit a locked build database, and clean reruns via `./scripts/build.sh` plus an isolated `-derivedDataPath build/DerivedData-direction-fix` test run were started with the new direction regressions passing during execution.

- [x] Improve captured record movement stroke detection sensitivity
  - Files: `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`, `ScratchLabDesktop/Services/MacCaptureEngine.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/CaptureReliabilityPhase1Tests.swift`
  - Done when: repeated baby scratch hand/record motion emits multiple truthful `recordMovementEvents` from live capture instead of collapsing most of the phrase into one long segment, direction changes split movement strokes instead of being merged away, jitter still fails closed without fake strokes, the corrected forward/back source mapping remains intact, captured notation continues to draw only from real movement events, audio-only partial notation stays direction-pending, and requested verification is run or any environment blocker is stated explicitly
  - Completed: root cause was in the movement segmentation path, not rendering. `MacCaptureEngine.RoutineDetectedNotationBuilder` only sampled positions on `HandMotionState` changes, so a full forward or backward pass was often measured from the start of one stroke to the delayed commit point of the opposite stroke. That collapsed real baby-scratch excursions into short deltas that later failed `RoutineNotationEventNormalizer` filtering, leaving 70+ audio events but only 1-3 captured movement strokes. Fixed by making the builder ingest every published live hand observation while routine recording is active, track each stroke's furthest confirmed position/time as it develops, finish the active stroke at that excursion when direction changes, and then start the next stroke from the new direction's first committed sample. Kept the existing `normalizedRecordDirection(forCameraSpaceDirection:)` source-of-truth fix in place, slightly lowered the normalizer's minimum stroke duration/position delta/speed thresholds to keep shorter real baby-scratch passes, and added regressions for alternating multi-stroke phrases, direction-change splitting, normal baby-scratch repetitions surviving suppression, jitter rejection, and source-level wiring to `recordObservation`. Verification so far: `xcodebuild -project ScratchLab.xcodeproj -scheme ScratchLabDesktop -destination 'platform=macOS' build` passed; focused builder regressions passed in isolated DerivedData; `python3 scripts/test_capture_pipeline.py` passed (`47/47`); `git diff --check` passed; the direct non-escalated full `xcodebuild ... test` run was blocked by sandbox write permissions to Xcode's default DerivedData/XCBuildData path, and an escalated rerun of the same required command is in progress with the broad suite already executing and the new movement regressions passing.

- [x] Diagnose and fix manual baby scratch capture still collapsing to one record movement event
  - Files: `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`, `ScratchLabDesktop/Services/MacCaptureEngine.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktop/Views/MacAnalyzerView.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/CaptureReliabilityPhase1Tests.swift`
  - Done when: real routine capture surfaces where movement is lost between camera frames, hand observations, normalized directions, builder samples, raw movement events, and final saved `recordMovementEvents`; repeated baby scratches no longer disappear behind over-sparse hand-pose sampling or trust-only filtering; DEBUG diagnostics report drop reasons without shipping user-facing clutter; corrected forward/back mapping stays intact; captured notation still draws only real movement events; audio-only takes remain direction-pending; and the requested build/test/script/diff verification passes
  - Completed: manual capture was still losing motion in two places upstream of notation. First, `MacCaptureEngine` only ran hand-pose analysis every `0.12s`, which is too sparse for repeated baby-scratch reversals and starved `HandDirectionTracker`/`RoutineDetectedNotationBuilder` of samples during live routine recording. Second, `RoutineNotationFusionEngine.snapshot(...)` was saving only trust-gated directional motion into the final snapshot path, so truthful normalized motion could still be thrown away before `recordMovementEvents` reached review/export. Fixed by adding a dedicated `routineRecordingHandPoseInterval` of `0.04s`, preserving the corrected `normalizedRecordDirection(forCameraSpaceDirection:)` mapping, saving truthful normalized `candidateMovementEvents` into the detected-notation snapshot while still using trusted fused motion only to decide whether notation is `detected` versus `partial`, and adding DEBUG-only movement diagnostics that count frames, observations, raw/semantic direction changes, builder samples, raw/normalized/fused/final movement events, and reason-specific drops. Surfaced that summary in the existing macOS Performance Diagnostics card and added regressions for truthful partial motion preservation, diagnostics/source wiring, jitter rejection, alternating multi-stroke builder output, renderer honesty, and audio-only behavior. Verification: `xcodebuild -project ScratchLab.xcodeproj -scheme ScratchLabDesktop -destination 'platform=macOS' build` passed; `xcodebuild -project ScratchLab.xcodeproj -scheme ScratchLabDesktop -destination 'platform=macOS' test` passed with `374` XCTest cases and `35` Swift Testing cases; `python3 scripts/test_capture_pipeline.py` passed (`47/47`); `git diff --check` passed.

- [x] Fix Baby Scratch coach notation timebase
  - Files: `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`, `ScratchLab/Models/CaptureCore.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/CaptureReliabilityPhase1Tests.swift`

- [x] Fix the shared iOS launch scheme so foreground Debug runs do not request undeclared background/location launch behavior
  - Files: `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`, `ScratchLab.xcodeproj/xcshareddata/xcschemes/ScratchLab.xcscheme`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/CaptureReliabilityPhase1Tests.swift`

Fixed by removing `allowLocationSimulation = "YES"` and the `LocationScenarioReference` block from `ScratchLab.xcodeproj/xcshareddata/xcschemes/ScratchLab.xcscheme`, leaving the launch style as normal foreground Debug, and adding a source-level regression in `ScratchLabDesktopTests/CaptureReliabilityPhase1Tests.swift` to keep the shared scheme free of location simulation and wait-for-executable launch settings. Verification: direct `xcodebuild -project ScratchLab.xcodeproj -scheme ScratchLab -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' build` passed, simulator `xcodebuild -project ScratchLab.xcodeproj -scheme ScratchLab -destination 'platform=iOS Simulator,name=iPhone 17' build` passed, `./scripts/build.sh` passed end-to-end, and the built simulator app installed plus launched on simulator `53B855D2-2933-4A9C-BB75-1AC5D866701E`. Physical-phone install verification was attempted on device `K` but blocked because the phone was locked.

- [x] Fix iOS guided-capture system-check overflow and safe-area navigation overlap
  - Files: `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`, `ScratchLab/Views/CompanionCameraView.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/CaptureReliabilityPhase1Tests.swift`
  - Done when: the guided-capture shell respects iPhone safe areas, the back action sits in system navigation chrome instead of being covered by in-content UI, the system-check action stack stays reachable on smaller screens, focused regressions pass, and `./scripts/build.sh` passes
  - Completed: root cause was that `CompanionCameraView` rendered its back/trailing controls inside the content stack while only padding for the raw safe-area inset, and `SystemCheckView` used a fixed vertical stack with no scroll container. On smaller iPhones that left the custom header competing with system navigation space and pushed lower system-check buttons off-screen. Fixed by moving the guided-capture back/trailing controls into toolbar-safe navigation chrome, increasing bottom safe-area padding in the shared capture shell, making `SystemCheckView` scrollable, and adding source-level regressions that pin the toolbar-safe navigation contract and scrollable system-check layout. Verification: the focused guided-capture source tests passed, and `./scripts/build.sh` passed end-to-end on this machine.

- [x] Revise the session cleanup policy so the UI stays capped without deleting real user work
  - Files: `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`, `ScratchLab/Models/CaptureCore.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/CaptureReliabilityPhase1Tests.swift`
  - Done when: the shared session presentation keeps `Recent Sessions` capped at three, the macOS routine draft store prunes only stale empty or abandoned setup-only drafts, completed/exported/artifact-backed sessions remain accessible through `All Sessions`, focused retention regressions pass, and `./scripts/build.sh` passes
  - Completed: root cause was that the previous retention patch treated sidebar clutter as a storage-retention problem and normalized the macOS routine draft store down to three total sessions. That was too destructive because completed, exported, or artifact-backed sessions could be deleted solely for exceeding the UI cap. Fixed by narrowing `SessionListPolicy` to a presentation-only recent-session limit, replacing total-count pruning in `RoutineSessionStore` with stale-draft pruning based on session age and meaningful activity, retaining sessions that have recorded take metrics, routine capture artifacts, or persisted upload jobs, and extending the regression suite to prove real sessions survive beyond the recent-session cap while stale empty drafts are still cleaned up. Verification: the focused session-retention test slice passed, and `./scripts/build.sh` passed end-to-end on this machine.

- [x] Refactor the shared session list/sidebar presentation so macOS and iOS stop stacking unbounded session entries
  - Files: `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`, `ScratchLab/Models/CaptureCore.swift`, `ScratchLab/Views/CompanionCameraView.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktop/Views/MacAnalyzerView.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/CaptureReliabilityPhase1Tests.swift`
  - Done when: session selection and listing routes through one shared `SessionListPresentationModel`, macOS shows a fixed `Active Session` section plus `Recent Sessions` and collapsed `All Sessions`, iOS setup shows `Current Session`, `Recent Sessions`, and collapsed `All Sessions` without the old single `Reuse Last Setup` path, opening a session updates shared last-opened ordering, setup/practice flows stop auto-creating new guided-capture sessions during setup-only transitions, and `./scripts/build.sh` passes
  - Completed: root cause was that macOS rendered the routine draft store as a flat append-only list while iOS only exposed a one-off `Reuse Last Setup` shortcut, and neither surface shared a canonical notion of `active`, `recent`, or `all` sessions. The app also had no shared open-history tracking, so session ordering was inconsistent, and guided capture refreshed session identity during setup-only transitions instead of reserving new-session boundaries for explicit user action or the later recording path. Fixed by adding a shared `SessionListPresentationModel` plus `SessionOpenHistoryStore` in Capture Core, routing the macOS sidebar and iOS guided-capture setup through that shared presentation, pinning macOS to a fixed `Active Session` card with `Recent Sessions` and collapsed `All Sessions`, replacing iOS `Reuse Last Setup` with `Current Session`, `Recent Sessions`, and collapsed `All Sessions`, and updating guided capture so setup-only transitions stop auto-rolling new session IDs while the drill-change reset defers its new identity until recording begins. Verification: focused session-presentation regressions passed, the full `ScratchLabDesktop` XCTest plan passed, and `./scripts/build.sh` passed end-to-end.

  - Files: `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`, `ScratchLabDesktop/ScratchLabDesktopApp.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktop/Views/MacAnalyzerView.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/CaptureReliabilityPhase1Tests.swift`
  - Done when: the macOS Practice card visibly exposes `No Beat` / `Beat On`, visible beat-mode selection, BPM controls, and `Play Beat` / `Stop Beat` before starting Practice; every control uses the shared `PracticeBeatStore` instead of a new local audio path; practice beat playback stops on Practice tab exit, app inactive/background, and recording-flow start; source-level regression coverage pins the desktop store injection plus the visible Mac Practice control contract; and `./scripts/build.sh` passes

Fixed by injecting the existing shared `PracticeBeatStore` into `ScratchLabDesktopApp`, adding a visible Practice beat section to the Mac Practice card with direct `No Beat` / `Beat On`, beat-mode buttons, BPM controls, and `Play Beat` / `Stop Beat`, and stopping the shared beat on Practice tab exit, app inactive, and recording-flow start. Verification: focused desktop Practice regressions passed, the new desktop source-contract tests passed, and `./scripts/build.sh` passed end-to-end.

- [x] Fix the iOS Practice setup beat-style selector so alternate practice beat modes are actually selectable
  - Files: `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`, `ScratchLab/Views/PracticeModeView.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/CaptureReliabilityPhase1Tests.swift`
  - Done when: the Scratch Practice setup screen on iOS exposes direct selectable beat-mode controls wired to the shared `PracticeBeatStore`, changing from `Click track` to other practice beat modes updates the selected state without requiring a new local audio path, focused Practice regressions cover mode switching plus the visible setup control contract, and `./scripts/build.sh` passes

In live use that menu rendered but did not reliably invoke `practiceBeatStore.selectBeatMode(...)`, so the user could start the click track but could not switch to `Boom Bap Trainer`, `Minimal Funk`, or `Battle Loop`. Fixed by replacing the setup-card menu with direct selectable beat-mode buttons bound to the shared store, keeping the existing playback/BPM path unchanged, adding a regression that mode changes restart the shared engine with the newly selected mode, and extending the source-level setup-overlay contract test to cover the direct mode controls. Verification: focused Practice tests passed, `./scripts/build.sh` passed end-to-end, and a rebuilt iPhone 17 simulator run confirmed `Click track` switched to `Boom Bap Trainer`, `Play Beat` toggled to `Stop Beat`, and `No Beat` immediately disabled playback.

- [x] Add optional shared beat/metronome playback to the Scratch Practice tab without creating capture/export artifacts
  - Files: `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`, `ScratchLab/Models/CaptureCore.swift`, `ScratchLab/ScratchLabApp.swift`, `ScratchLab/Views/CompanionCameraView.swift`, `ScratchLab/Views/PracticeModeView.swift`, `ScratchLab/Views/LevelSelectView.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/CaptureReliabilityPhase1Tests.swift`
  - Done when: Scratch Practice can play optional beat guidance using the existing shared timing engine without creating takes, exporting, or starting capture sessions; Practice exposes beat on/off, BPM, no-beat, and play/stop controls; beat playback stops on Practice exit, app background/inactive, and record-flow start; shared scratch/BPM plus compatible beat mode can prefill Record without overriding stored record defaults; and `./scripts/build.sh` passes

That meant Practice could not reuse Record's BPM defaults/range and beat engine, had no single lifecycle owner to stop practice playback on tab/app/record transitions, and could not cleanly carry the selected shared timing settings into Record. Fixed by adding a shared `PracticeBeatStore` in Capture Core backed by `ScratchLabBeatEngine` and `CaptureClickTrackDefaults`, wiring it through the app environment, using it from the Practice setup UI and guided-drill timing, stopping it on Practice/app/record lifecycle changes, and letting Record prefill from Practice only when there are no stored Record defaults. Test results: the six focused Practice/Record regressions passed and `./scripts/build.sh` passed end-to-end.

- [x] Fix confirmed live export blockers for calibration validation and selected-take timing metadata resolution
  - Files: `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`, `scripts/test_capture_pipeline.py`, `ScratchLab/Services/SessionExportCoordinator.swift`, `ScratchLab/Views/CompanionCameraView.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/CaptureReliabilityPhase1Tests.swift`

- [x] Validate DVS Rane ONE MKII motion decode end-to-end on real hardware
  - Files: `ScratchLab/Models/TimecodeCMSampleBufferAdapter.swift`, `ScratchLab/Models/TimecodeControlPipeline.swift`, `ScratchLab/Models/TimecodeDecodedFrame.swift`, `ScratchLab/Models/TimecodeMotionStabilityFilter.swift`, `ScratchLab/Models/TimecodePhaseDecoder.swift`, `ScratchLab/Models/TimecodeValidationSnapshot.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktop/Views/TimecodeControlCard.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktop/Views/DVSControlVinylPanel.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/DVSControlVinylAnalyzerTests.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/TimecodeStabilityFilterTests.swift`, `ScratchLabDesktopTests/TimecodePrototypeProfileTests.swift`, `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`
  - Done when: Auto DVS pair selection correctly rejects a loud non-timecode pair (13/14) and selects the real quadrature pair (5/6) on real Rane ONE MKII hardware, the confidence-acceptance gate actually honors the UI's Min confidence setting instead of a hardcoded threshold, a user-customized Min confidence and Invert direction survive app relaunch, physical forward platter motion reports `forward` (not `backward`), live-captured accepted motion samples are non-zero, and none of this requires enabling playback, scoring, MIDI, notation routing, or audio output routing
  - Completed: root-caused in stages against live Rane ONE MKII (14ch Int32 interleaved @ 48000 Hz) hardware, not synthetic fixtures alone. (1) Auto pair scoring picked 13/14 purely on RMS/peak despite it having zero dominant frequency / zero-crossing rate on both channels — added a carrier-evidence gate (plausible 500-2500 Hz band + minimum zero-crossing rate) so a loud pair with no real carrier is rejected as `no timecode carrier`; confirmed live: Auto now selects 5/6 and rejects 13/14. (2) With carrier selection fixed, live motion still showed `accepted: 0` — found `TimecodeControlMode.diagnosticsOnly` never runs `TimecodePhaseDecoder` by design; `controlPrototype` mode is required to exercise the actual decoder. (3) Even in Control Prototype, all frames were rejected `low_confidence` — found the stability filter's confidence gate was hardcoded to 0.3 and never read the UI's `pipeline.minConfidence`; real Rane 5/6 confidence sits around 0.117-0.155, structurally below any default. Wired the gate to the live `minConfidence` value. (4) A user-set Min confidence (e.g. 0.10) was being silently reset to the active preset's fixed constant on every relaunch; added persisted override storage plus an auto-switch to the existing `.manual` profile on direct user edits, confirmed the setting now survives relaunch. (5) With motion decoding, physical forward platter spin reported `backward`; confirmed the existing `Invert direction` toggle was already fully wired end-to-end and just needed the same relaunch-persistence fix — with it ON, forward spin now reports `forward`. (6) Cleaned up and stabilized the DVS control panel's layout (fixed-height rows instead of appearing/disappearing blocks, collapsed disclosure for verbose per-channel/per-pair diagnostics) without touching any decoding/calibration/logger behavior. Final live-validated snapshot: signal health `usable`, signal class `quadratureCandidate`, phase offset ≈ -81°, ZCR freq 986/986 Hz, Min conf UI/gate 0.100/0.100, frames raw/pre/post-filter 75/74/74, accepted 1382, direction `forward`, raw rate 0.118 u/s, smoothed rate 0.069 u/s, status `usablePrototypeControl`. Playback override, scoring, MIDI, notation routing, and audio output routing were not touched or enabled at any point in this work. Committed as `2f80778`, `28c125f`, `862d3b8` (plus the earlier multichannel-extraction foundation in `cf7953f`, `9f3bd9a`, `6bb2b5c`).

- [x] Preserve/verify Rane DVS profile defaults or user-facing setup instructions
  - Done when: the validated Rane ONE MKII configuration (DVS pair Auto, Min confidence 0.10, Invert direction OFF with correctly oriented RCA wiring) is easy for a user to reproduce without re-deriving it from scratch — either as a documented setup note or a dedicated profile/preset
  - Completed: this task's own wording describes intermediate values from mid-arc hardware history — right after Karl physically corrected reversed RCA wiring, before a real internal sign-convention mismatch was found between the live diagnostic classifier and the actual decoder. Every hardware session since (dozens of entries across `AI_HANDOFF.md`/`DEV_LOG.md`) confirmed the actual final, validated, and already-preserved configuration is: physical pair **3/4 pinned** (not Auto — pinning was itself a later, more robust fix protecting against pair auto-selection instability during a stop/reversal), Invert direction **ON** (not OFF — audible forward playback is ground truth, not either diagnostic's raw phase-sign label), and Min confidence 0.10. All three values were already correctly encoded in `TimecodePrototypeProfile.raneOneMkiiDebug()` and `TimecodeControlCard.applyPreset`, and the profile's own defaults were already regression-tested (`testRaneOneMkiiDebugProfileMatchesValidatedHardwareDefaults`). What was missing: (1) the physical-pair-pinning logic lived only as an inline ternary in a private SwiftUI method, exercised solely by hand during live hardware sessions — extracted to a pure, directly testable `TimecodeControlCard.channelPairSelection(for:)` and added `testRaneOneMkiiDebugPresetPinsPhysicalPair3And4`/`testNonRanePresetsRestoreAutoPairSelection`; (2) the parts a Swift preset's values can't capture on their own (which physical USB pair to use, how to read an apparent forward/backward mismatch) were only discoverable by searching scattered hardware-session history — added a `TimecodeControlPreset.setupNote` shown directly in the preset UI whenever the Rane preset is selected, with `testRaneOneMkiiDebugPresetHasSetupNoteOtherPresetsDoNot`; (3) the doc comments on the preset enum case and `raneOneMkiiDebug()` said only "validated ... this session" (stale, undated, and silent about the superseded Invert-OFF belief) — expanded both to state plainly that Invert ON is correct and final, and explain why, so a future session doesn't "fix" it back to OFF. No decoder threshold, calibration value, direction logic, playback behavior, or any DVS numeric default was changed — this was verification plus making the already-correct configuration provably reproducible and durably documented. Verification: `TimecodePrototypeProfileTests` + `TimecodeLiveTapTests` + `DVSControlVinylAnalyzerTests` + `TimecodeDecoderTests` + `ScratchSamplePlaybackControllerTests` + `DVSOfflineGrainRendererTests` passed `208/208` combined in both scheme configurations; direct Debug macOS build and a direct iOS build (code signing disabled to bypass the same unrelated local watch-companion provisioning-profile mismatch as prior slices) both succeeded; `python3 scripts/test_capture_pipeline.py` passed `47/47`; `scripts/build.sh` reproduced exactly the established unrelated baseline (Advanced diagnostics, MIDI learn/clear, Coach pause, notation source-contract) with no new failures before stopping via its own `set -e`; `git diff --check` passed. - [x] Map validated prototype motion to notation/playback as a separate later phase
  - Done when: there is an explicit, separately-scoped plan (not started as part of this arc) for routing decoded DVS motion into notation and/or playback, kept behind the same safety gates already in place
  - Completed: ChatGPT (architect) supplied the explicit phased plan below via `<<<PLAN...PLAN>>>`. This is planning only — no production code, routing, UI toggle, persistence, or export change was written; nothing was committed, staged, pushed, reset, or discarded. Full plan text is preserved verbatim in `AI_HANDOFF.md`.
    - **Architecture decision:** playback and notation stay fully independent. The existing `TimecodePlaybackBridge` prototype playback path remains default OFF and unrelated. A future notation route must NOT reuse `playbackDriveEnabled` as its gate, must NOT auto-enable `validationOverride`, and this planning slice adds no production routing/UI/persistence/export.
    - **Planned notation route:** a separate DEBUG/macOS-only `TimecodeNotationBridge` (future slice) with its own `notationRoutingEnabled` flag defaulting false, consuming the stabilized `TimecodeControlPipeline` output directly (not the audio playback controller). Same fail-closed checks as playback — Control Prototype mode, Live Tap active/fresh, usable signal, confidence above threshold, known direction, valid platter timeline, validation passed, no replay conflict — sharing pure trust-decision logic with playback where practical so the two bridges can't silently diverge on safety rules, while keeping their enable flags and outputs independent.
    - **Recording design:** accumulate only trusted `.timecodeLive` `PlatterPositionTimeline` samples while a routine take actively records; rebase to the take-relative clock; dedupe overlapping pipeline windows by timestamp; preserve monotonic timestamps, unbounded signed position, conservative confidence; never extrapolate through stale/missing/stopped/unknown-direction intervals. At take finalization, run the accumulated timeline through the existing pure `derivePrimitives(from:)` grammar, convert direction segments to `DetectedNotationRecordMovementEvent` via one pure adapter, normalize positions for the existing captured-notation renderer while retaining the raw timeline for diagnostics, and tag provenance `source: "timecode_live"` throughout snapshot/logs/export.
    - **Source precedence:** when notation routing is enabled and a trusted DVS timeline exists, DVS is authoritative for platter direction/travel; camera tracking may continue for hand-position diagnostics/overlays but its movement events must never be merged with DVS movement events; audio/fader events remain separately fusable as today; with no trusted DVS movement, fail closed (no notation samples) rather than inventing/blending motion — any camera fallback must be a separately defined, user-visible policy, never an implicit one.
    - **Required future implementation slices** (recorded as the five new tasks immediately below, in order): (1) pure trust gate/accumulator/adapter with saved-fixture tests, no UI; (2) DEBUG-only capture lifecycle wiring with an independent default-off toggle; (3) snapshot/export provenance and deterministic DVS-vs-camera precedence; (4) saved-fixture validation across `slow_reversals`/`fast_reversals`/`baby_scratch`/`start_stop`/`needle_lift`; (5) one final hardware validation only after all offline tests pass.
    - **Regression requirements carried into every future slice:** both playback and notation routing default OFF; enabling notation never enables playback or validation override, and vice versa; bad/stale/low-confidence/unknown-direction input produces no notation samples; stops and needle lifts create honest gaps, not synthetic strokes; forward/backward fixture motion creates correctly oriented events; DVS and camera movement are never double-counted; timecode provenance survives UI, snapshot, persistence/logging, and export; release/App Store behavior is unchanged until separately approved.

  - Done when: a pure trust-decision function exists for notation routing, sharing its fail-closed logic with `TimecodePlaybackBridge` where practical (Control Prototype mode, Live Tap active and fresh, usable signal, confidence above the active threshold, known direction, valid platter timeline, validation passed, no replay conflict) but with its own independent `notationRoutingEnabled` flag (default false) whose output never depends on and never sets `playbackDriveEnabled`; a pure accumulator collects only trusted `.timecodeLive` `PlatterPositionTimeline` samples while a routine take is actively recording, rebased to the take-relative clock, deduplicated across overlapping pipeline windows by timestamp, preserving monotonic timestamps/unbounded signed position/conservative confidence, and never extrapolating through stale/missing/stopped/unknown-direction intervals; a pure adapter converts the accumulated timeline through the existing `derivePrimitives(from:)` grammar into `DetectedNotationRecordMovementEvent` values tagged `source: "timecode_live"`; deterministic tests cover trust-gate pass/fail per condition, accumulation/dedup/rebasing, and timeline-to-event conversion; no UI toggle, persistence, export change, or wiring into any live capture path; nothing committed.
  - Completed: added three new pure files in `ScratchLab/Models/` (compiled into both the `ScratchLab` and `ScratchLabDesktop` targets, matching every other Timecode model file): `TimecodeNotationTrustGate.swift` (`TimecodeNotationTrustDecision` enum + `TimecodeNotationTrustGate.evaluate(...)`, mirroring `TimecodePlaybackBridge.evaluate`'s exact 8-gate order/thresholds but holding no state, sharing no enable flag, and never touching `playbackDriveEnabled`), `TimecodeNotationTimelineAccumulator.swift` (value-type accumulator: rebase-to-take-relative-clock, cutoff-based dedup that also enforces strict monotonicity, confidence/position carried through unchanged, no interpolation/extrapolation ever — a real gap between two appended windows survives as a real time jump), and `TimecodeNotationEventAdapter.swift` (converts a finalized timeline through the existing internal `derivePrimitives(from:)` grammar into `CaptureCore.DetectedNotationRecordMovementEvent`s tagged `source: "timecode_live"`, emitting only `.directionSegment` primitives — idle holds and reversals are not emitted as movement events). Key finding from those fixture tests: the full fail-closed trust gate (matching every one of the playback bridge's own safety checks) is intentionally far more conservative than the raw per-tick `pipeline.currentDirection` check `TimecodeFixtureValidationTests` already uses — on these short (0.24s) excerpts, only one side of a reversal reliably accumulates enough trusted, contiguous samples to form a full grammar `DirectionSegment` on both sides is not guaranteed. Assertions were scoped to what the excerpt length actually supports (at least one real, correctly-tagged event; both directions only for the longer `baby_scratch` excerpt where the full chain does clear that bar) rather than weakened arbitrarily — documented in the test file's own comments so a future reader understands why. No UI toggle, capture-lifecycle wiring, persistence, or export change was added — `notationRoutingEnabled` exists only as an explicit parameter callers must pass to `evaluate(...)`, not a stored/exposed setting anywhere. Nothing enables playback or notation routing in production; `playbackDriveEnabled` and this new pure logic remain fully independent, verified by `testTrustGateNeverReadsOrTouchesPlaybackBridgeState`. The retry needed to use the main Routine Capture Start Recording control (not Advanced → Capture Details → Start Dataset Capture), keep trusted DVS motion active throughout a short take, and inspect the resulting `_routine.json` for `timecode_live`.
  - Completed: ran that retry. `notationRoutingEnabled` was flipped on for one launch only via a temporary, uncommitted `#if DEBUG` env-var check added to `MacAnalyzerView.onAppear` (`SCRATCHLAB_ENABLE_DVS_NOTATION`) — the stored property's own default (`false`) was never touched, so no other debug run or any XCTest run was affected. Karl connected his physical Rane ONE MKII with the existing timecode-vinyl setup (same rig used to capture the slices 1–4 fixtures), got the Setup Checklist fully green including "Validation passed," and recorded 3 routine takes of real baby-scratch motion via the main Routine Capture Start Recording control (session `session_2026_07_31_unknown_performer_baby_scratch_95_bpm`, 18.3s/14.4s/26.9s takes). All 3 exported takes showed `detectionSources: ["audio", "timecode_live"]` and real, correctly alternating forward/backward `recordMovementEvents` (10/14/17 events) — DVS routing worked end-to-end on live hardware, trust-gated and validated exactly as designed, with playback override left off throughout (independence held).
  - Bug found and fixed via this real session (exactly what slice 5 exists to catch): every exported `recordMovementEvents[].source` read `"video"` despite `detectionSources` correctly showing `"timecode_live"`. Root cause: `RoutineNotationFusionEngine.fuseMovementEvents` (`MacCaptureEngine.swift`) unconditionally rebuilds every motion event it emits with a hardcoded `source: "video"` (unmatched events) or `"fused"` (events paired with a burst audio event), discarding whatever source the input event actually carried — a pre-existing assumption from before DVS notation existed, when camera was the only possible motion source. Slice 3's `withTimecodeLiveMovementProvenance()` only ever patched the top-level `detectionSources` label, never each event's own `.source` field, so this gap was invisible to every slice 1–4 test — all of which exercised precedence/provenance directly and never through the real `RoutineNotationFusionEngine().snapshot(...)` call path `MacCaptureEngine.finalizeRoutineRecording` actually uses. Fixed by extending `CaptureCore.DetectedNotationSnapshot.withTimecodeLiveMovementProvenance()` (still the same pure, additive, post-processing method — `RoutineNotationFusionEngine`'s own internals remain untouched) to also retag every event in `recordMovementEvents` to `source: "timecode_live"`, since its only call site (`notationRoutingEnabled && lastDrainedTimecodeNotationTimeline != nil`) guarantees every event in that snapshot originated from DVS, never camera. Updated `testWithTimecodeLiveMovementProvenanceReplacesVideoNotAudio` to assert the corrected behavior (per-event source now changes too, all other event fields — time/position/direction/confidence — survive unchanged). Karl's already-exported session did **not** need to be redone: the bug was purely a mislabeled provenance string, not a data-correctness issue — the direction/timing/position data was always correct, confirmed independently via `session_review.json`'s `directionConflictDetected: false` and sane `directionFlipCount` values (8/11/15) across all 3 takes.
  - Verification: `TimecodeNotationRoutingTests` 42/42 (1 expected skip — grew to 42 from a parallel Codex session's `TimecodeNotationDiagnostics` counter tests, unrelated to this fix); a broad regression pass (`CaptureReliabilityPhase1CoreTests` 628 combined tests across both scheme passes + nine more DVS-adjacent suites) exactly matching the established baseline (8 failures/1 skip both passes, nothing new — `testTimedClickExportMetadataPersistsPresetBPMsAndTiming`, the environmental flake investigated in slice 3, passed cleanly in both passes here too); direct iOS build (code signing disabled to bypass the same unrelated local watch-companion provisioning-profile mismatch as prior slices) succeeded; `git diff --check` clean. Nothing was committed, staged, or pushed.
  - Post-session cleanup: the temporary `MacAnalyzerView.swift` env-var toggle (`SCRATCHLAB_ENABLE_DVS_NOTATION`) has been reverted — `onAppear` is back to exactly its pre-slice-5 form, with no trace of the toggle left anywhere in the file. Re-verified clean afterward: `xcodebuild build-for-testing` succeeded, and a full broad regression pass (`CaptureReliabilityPhase1CoreTests` 628 combined tests across both scheme passes + nine more DVS-adjacent suites, including `TimecodeNotationRoutingTests` 42/42 with 1 skip) matched the established baseline exactly in both passes (8 failures/1 skip, nothing new) — confirming the revert didn't disturb anything and the actual DVS provenance fix in `CaptureCore.swift` remains fully intact. `git diff --check` clean. Full-capture measurements locate the first density limit before notation: 4,800 frames at 48 kHz produce about 10 callbacks/sec, and the decoder emits at most one frame per callback. Steady/position captures therefore reach 9.6–10.0 trusted samples/sec, while within-callback direction changes reduce `fast_reversals` to 2.73 post-filter samples/sec, `fine_flicks` to 1.75/sec, and `baby_scratch` to 2.74/sec before the trust gate. A deterministic expected-failure regression also proves `TimecodePlatterAdapter.adapt` resets cumulative position on every flush: two valid steady windows end at `0.1000` instead of the integrated `0.2000`. The decoder's fixed `velocity * 0.1` displacement likewise assumes a 100 ms callback. No production behavior changed. - [x] Design and measure an offline-only stop-transition reversal-confirmation rule
  - Files: `ScratchLabDesktopTests/TimecodeFixtureValidationTests.swift`, local hardware fixture evidence, workflow documentation only
  - Done when: candidate offline confirmation rules (minimum coherent reverse displacement, consecutive-window confirmation, near-stop hysteresis, and a narrow combination) are measured against `start_stop`, `needle_lift`, `slow_reversals`, `fast_reversals`, `fine_flicks`, `baby_scratch`, and the steady/one-way fixtures at the established 1024-frame candidate window; each candidate reports false-transition removal, genuine reversal retention, added latency, trusted density, position-integration error, spike/dropout impact, and processing cost; no production cadence, threshold, or live decoding path is changed; focused tests, `python3 scripts/test_capture_pipeline.py`, `scripts/build.sh all`, and `git diff --check` are run; nothing is committed.
  - Completed: added a deterministic, test-only `DVSReversalConfirmationCandidate` state machine plus a shared-decode audit (`decodeDVSFrameStream` decodes each fixture once at 1024 frames; `auditDVSReversalConfirmation` evaluates every candidate cheaply against that single decode) to `TimecodeFixtureValidationTests.swift`. Four candidates were measured, matching the plan's list plus one narrow combination: `minReverseDisplacement(0.15)`, `consecutiveWindows(2)`/`consecutiveWindows(3)`, `nearStopHysteresis(0.15)`, and `combined(floor=0.15, windows=2)`, against a `none` baseline reproducing current 1024-frame output unchanged. An early version of the shared decode helper had a bug — it read `pipeline.latestDecodeResult` unconditionally instead of gating on `flushDecode()`'s own return value, so stale frames from a prior successful window were silently re-appended on every subsequent quiet window. This inflated `start_stop`'s raw backward count from the previously established 28 to 310 before it was caught by cross-checking against the already-validated subwindow audit baseline and fixed to match `auditDVSSubwindow`'s exact gating pattern; the corrected rerun reproduced the established `start_stop` (28 false backward) and `needle_lift` (1 false backward) raw counts exactly.
  - No single candidate is safe to ship. `minReverseDisplacement(0.15)` is the only one that fully removes every false reverse in `start_stop` (28/28) and `needle_lift` (1/1), but it destroys 62-79% of genuine direction changes in `slow_reversals` (45→17), `fine_flicks` (76→16), and `baby_scratch` (292→72) — the exact scratch techniques the app must recognize — because genuine fast/fine reversals are frequently just as small in per-frame displacement as the false ones. `consecutiveWindows(2)`/`(3)` are weak where needed (remove only 2/28 and 6/28 `start_stop` false samples) while unexpectedly destroying 58% of `slow_reversals`' genuine changes (45→19): rejecting brief single-frame runs merges adjacent same-direction output, collapsing many short genuine back-and-forth changes at once. `nearStopHysteresis(0.15)` alone is the best-preserving candidate for genuine content (0% loss in `slow_reversals` and `baby_scratch`, ≤8% elsewhere, zero added latency) and removes 86% of `start_stop`'s false reverses (28→4), but the residual sample at 12.821s and `needle_lift`'s single false sample both carry velocity above the floor and survive unchanged (0% removed on `needle_lift`). `combined(floor=0.15, windows=2)` closes the `needle_lift` gap (1/1 removed) but reintroduces the consecutive-window fragmentation damage on top of the near-stop floor, cutting `slow_reversals` to the same 58%-lost level and `baby_scratch` to 18% lost (292→238) for no additional `start_stop` benefit over near-stop alone. Steady and one-way fixtures (`stationary`, `steady_normal`, `slow_forward`, `slow_backward`, `fast_forward`, `fast_backward`, `position_start/middle/end`) confirmed all four non-baseline candidates safely clear the small number of pre-existing false blips already known from the subwindow audit (1-4 samples each) without introducing new false transitions. Position-integration error scales with how much real motion a candidate discards, from 0.003-0.03 for the gentlest candidates up to 5.06 for `minReverseDisplacement` on `baby_scratch`. Decode cost is unchanged (0.033x-0.26x captured duration across fixtures, shared across all candidates); candidate evaluation itself is a negligible O(n) pass over the already-decoded stream.
  - Verification: the always-run excerpt test (`testHardwareTransitionExcerptsReportReversalConfirmationCandidates`, 6 checked-in 0.24s transition fixtures) passed in both scheme configurations. The opt-in full-capture audit (`testLocalFullHardwareCapturesReportReversalConfirmationCandidates`, gated on `SCRATCHLAB_RUN_FULL_DVS_REVERSAL_CONFIRMATION_AUDIT=1`, required `launchctl setenv`/`unsetenv` rather than a plain shell export because the macOS-destination test host is launched via Launch Services and does not inherit the invoking shell's environment) passed in both scheme configurations against all 15 full local captures x 6 candidates. `python3 scripts/test_capture_pipeline.py` passed 47/47. `git diff --check` passed. `scripts/build.sh all` ran both configurations; see the dated `DEV_LOG.md` entry for the exact baseline-failure confirmation from this run.
  - Follow-up notes: no production filter was implemented and no live decoding path, cadence, or threshold changed — this was measurement only, per the explicit instruction not to implement or switch on any candidate without Karl's separate approval. If a production slice is ever proposed, `nearStopHysteresis` is the only candidate whose genuine-content cost looks tolerable, but it alone is not safe (misses `needle_lift` entirely and 4/28 `start_stop` samples) and would need a different supplementary signal, not a blunter consecutive-window/displacement gate, to close the remaining gap. Nothing was committed, staged, or pushed.

- [x] Run an offline-only decoder-level phase-continuity audit before any reversal-confirmation rule
  - Files: `ScratchLabDesktopTests/TimecodeFixtureValidationTests.swift`, local hardware fixture evidence, workflow documentation only
  - Done when: raw stereo carrier phase-continuity signals (left/right carrier phase, cross-channel phase offset, unwrapped phase progression between adjacent windows, phase-offset stability/coherence, measured carrier frequency and observed cycle count, correlation magnitude, channel balance/RMS, and phase behavior immediately before/during/after a direction decision) are measured per-window and cross-window at the established 1024-frame candidate window across `start_stop`, `needle_lift`, `slow_reversals`, `fast_reversals`, `fine_flicks`, `baby_scratch`, steady normal, and slow/fast one-way forward/backward fixtures; the audit uses the synchronized-video findings already recorded in `AI_HANDOFF.md` as ground truth (not the assumption that every decoded direction in a reversal-labelled fixture is correct) to determine whether phase continuity separates genuine reversals from stop/catch/near-stop ambiguity; if measurements reveal a useful distinction, one or more narrowly defined phase-continuity decision candidates are tested (not assumed) and each reports false `start_stop`/`needle_lift` reverse samples/transitions removed, genuine reversal samples/direction changes retained, direction-change latency, steady/one-way accuracy, trusted sample density, position-integration error, spike/dropout impact, and processing cost relative to captured duration; no production decoder, callback size, live cadence, stability filter, trust gate, playback, notation routing, visualization, persistence, export, or confidence threshold changes, and no fixture label/video timestamp is hardcoded into production logic; if raw phase continuity does not cleanly separate the cases, that is concluded honestly instead of adding another heuristic; focused tests, the opt-in full-capture audit, `python3 scripts/test_capture_pipeline.py`, `scripts/build.sh all`, and `git diff --check` are run; nothing is committed.
  - Completed: added a test-only raw phase-continuity audit to `TimecodeFixtureValidationTests.swift` that duplicates `TimecodePhaseDecoder`'s private `extractPhaseDelta`/`zcrFrequency` math exactly (same silence/clipping/correlation/carrier gates, same ±30° dead band) so measurements are faithful to the real decoder without modifying `TimecodePhaseDecoder.swift`, but returns per-channel phase instead of folding it into one delta. Classified every 1024-frame window into `silenceOrClipped`/`lowConfidence`/`deadBand`/`decodable`, computed a best-fit unwrapped phase-step residual between consecutive measurable (`decodable` or `deadBand`) windows (trying both rotation signs since ZCR frequency carries no sign — a small residual means the carrier evolved coherently, a large one means it did not), then grouped the decodable stream into same-direction runs and attached to each run's first window: the entry-step residual, and the duration/count of contiguous non-decodable windows immediately preceding it ("preceding ambiguous dwell" — how long the signal sat in a low-confidence/dead-band/silent state before this direction was confirmed). Direction is calibrated with the same `invertDirection = true` every other fixture replay in this file already applies via `TimecodePrototypeProfile.raneOneMkiiDebug()` (an early run without this fix showed `position_start/middle/end` — single-carrier 1x-forward captures — decoding as uniformly "backward" against the raw, pre-calibration decoder math; applying the established inversion fixed it, confirming this was a calibration-convention mismatch in the audit, not a decoder bug).
  - Measured against all 15 full local captures at the established 1024-frame window: `start_stop` (42.379s) produced 19 false backward runs (47 raw decodable windows, before any stability filter — lower in the pipeline than the 28-post-filter count established by the prior reversal-confirmation audit, since this audit intentionally inspects raw decoder-level output only); `needle_lift` (44.288s) produced 1 false backward run. Compared against the genuine reversal-drill fixtures' transitions (`slow_reversals` 47, `fast_reversals` 54, `fine_flicks` 84, `baby_scratch` 307 — 492 genuine transitions total), no raw phase-continuity measure separates false from genuine: entry phase-step residual spans the same 0–180° range for both (genuine `fast_reversals` transitions range from 0.92° to 172.86°, so even a coherent, near-zero-residual crossing is not proof of a real reversal); correlation magnitude for `baby_scratch`'s genuine transitions is often lower than `start_stop`'s false ones (196/307 genuine `baby_scratch` transitions have correlation below 0.3, versus a 0.103–0.552 range for the 19 `start_stop` false runs); and preceding low-confidence/dead-band dwell overlaps almost completely — 16 of the 19 `start_stop` false runs have preceding dwell of 0–14 windows, entirely inside the range genuine `fine_flicks`/`slow_reversals` transitions themselves produce (max 14 and 11 windows respectively). Only 3 of the 19 `start_stop` false runs (dwell 21/39/84 windows = 0.45–1.79s) and `needle_lift`'s single false run (257 windows = 5.48s) fall outside that overlap — but those are multi-second, silence-adjacent spans already distinguishable by a plain duration/silence check, not by anything specific to phase continuity, and they account for a small minority of the false samples. Steady/one-way fixtures reproduced the same small number of pre-existing raw false blips already known (`slow_forward` 2, `slow_backward` 3, `fast_forward` 6, `fast_backward` 1 windows), consistent with prior audits. Audit processing cost stayed at 0.028x–0.041x captured duration across all 15 fixtures (~17s wall time total), negligible next to decode itself.
  - Conclusion: raw stereo carrier phase continuity does **not** cleanly separate genuine reversals from false `start_stop`/`needle_lift` reverses. The large majority of false samples are statistically indistinguishable on correlation, cycle count, entry-residual, or preceding-dwell from a genuine fast/fine-flick/baby-scratch reversal's own zero-crossing — both pass through the same low-correlation, low-cycle-count, briefly-incoherent phase state, because a true reversal's zero-crossing and a false stop/catch's near-stall moment look the same to the carrier. No candidate decision rule was implemented, per the explicit instruction to conclude honestly instead of adding another heuristic when the measurements do not support one.
  - Verification: the always-run focused excerpt test (`testHardwareTransitionExcerptsReportPhaseContinuityAudit`, 6 checked-in 0.24s + 3 position excerpts) passed in both scheme configurations (an early run without the direction-calibration fix above failed structurally-consistent-but-mislabelled; fixed before this pass). The opt-in full-capture audit (`testLocalFullHardwareCapturesReportPhaseContinuityAudit`, gated on `SCRATCHLAB_RUN_FULL_DVS_PHASE_CONTINUITY_AUDIT=1`, set via `launchctl setenv`/`unsetenv`) passed in both scheme configurations against all 15 full local captures. `python3 scripts/test_capture_pipeline.py` passed 47/47. `git diff --check` passed. `scripts/build.sh all` ran the full desktop XCTest plan in both test-plan configurations (2,258 tests; `set -euo pipefail` stops the script at the test stage, so the iOS/macOS/watchOS build legs were not reached in this run — the repo's normal behavior whenever the established baseline failures below are present) and exited 65 only on the same established unrelated Advanced diagnostics (`testAdvancedDiagnosticsExplainCameraAndTickActivity`), MIDI learn/receive/clear (`testReceivingMIDICCUpdatesLastMIDIEventSummary`, `testMIDILearnUpdatesFromListeningToLearnedStateWhenCCArrives`, `testClearCrossfaderMappingRemovesMapping`), and Coach pause (`testCoachPauseHoldsPoseFromAudioTimeAndAvoidsPausedTimelineWork`) failures; no new failure appeared and every new phase-continuity test passed in both configurations.
  - Follow-up notes: no production decoder, callback size, cadence, stability filter, trust gate, playback, notation routing, visualization, persistence, export, or confidence threshold changed — this was measurement only. The stubborn residual `start_stop` sample already flagged by the prior reversal-confirmation audit (near 12.8s, surviving `nearStopHysteresis` because it carries velocity above the floor) shows moderate correlation (0.49), a real measured cycle count (9.26), and near-zero entry residual on both channels here too — consistent with a genuine, briefly phase-locked backward carrier (most plausibly the platter physically rebounding a small amount at the instant of a hand catch), not decoder noise, which is exactly why no phase-based or velocity-based post-decode rule tested so far has been able to remove it without also destroying real fast-reversal content. No further post-decode heuristic is recommended. Nothing was committed, staged, or pushed.

It holds direction `.unknown` (indeterminate) until a window is "strong" (category `.decodable`, phase margin beyond the ±30° dead-band ≥ `marginDeg`, correlation ≥ `minCorrelation`, channel balance ≥ `minBalance`, cycle count ≥ `minCycles`) — entry into indeterminate is immediate on any single disqualifying or opposite-direction window (easy to enter), while (re)committing a direction requires `exitWindows` consecutive strong, same-direction windows (asymmetric hysteresis, harder to leave). Baseline reuses the already-verified `phaseSteps`/`phaseRuns` helpers on the identical decoded window stream for an apples-to-apples comparison (reproduced the phase-continuity audit's established numbers exactly: `start_stop` 19 false/18 genuine forward runs, `needle_lift` 1 false/1 genuine run, `slow_reversals` 47/`fast_reversals` 54/`fine_flicks` 84/`baby_scratch` 307 genuine transitions, 492 total).

Adding margin/correlation/balance/cycle-count gates on top (the `margin=10+` candidates) removes more false content (`start_stop` down to 1 residual sample, 0 at `margin≥20`) but costs sharply more genuine content specifically in `baby_scratch` (307→90 at `margin=10`, a further 60-point drop versus the `exit=2`-only isolation) and `fine_flicks` (84→66→52→26 across the three combined candidates), because ScratchLab's fastest, lowest-amplitude technique produces plenty of genuine strokes with the same low margin/correlation/balance signature as the false near-zero samples. `fast_reversals` was the one fixture that stayed comparatively cheap (54→52/44/34, at most 37% lost even at the most aggressive setting). All candidate genuine runs matched a nearby (≤2s) baseline run (`unmatchedGenuine=0` everywhere), i.e. the loss is consistently a merge/suppression of real transitions, not spurious new events; measured acquisition latency after a successful (re)commit stayed low (≤0.32s across every candidate/fixture pair).

No candidate is recommended for production. This is now the fourth independent signal family (displacement, timing/hysteresis, raw phase continuity, and now an explicit consecutive-window/margin-gated indeterminate state) that fails to cleanly separate this specific false-positive class from genuine fast/fine reversal content using audio-only features.

  - Architecture comparison (from reading upstream 1.9 source, no code copied into this repo): xwax is a true **per-sample streaming state machine** (`struct timecoder` persists continuously across every `timecoder_submit()` call, no per-window batching), decoding an **LFSR-encoded absolute bitstream** (20-bit Serato/Traktor/Pioneer definitions with seed/taps/length constants, forward/reverse LFSR functions, and a hash-chained lookup table built once at startup mapping every one of ~700k–950k possible LFSR states back to an absolute vinyl position). Direction comes from the *instantaneous* relative polarity of two channels' zero-crossings (an adaptive per-channel EMA "zero" baseline plus a **fixed absolute amplitude threshold**, i.e. a literal Schmitt-trigger hysteresis band in the raw waveform domain) and only updates on an actual crossing event — if nothing crosses zero, direction is simply left unchanged rather than recomputed, unlike ScratchLab's decoder which always emits a fresh phase-fit and direction guess every 1024-frame window whether or not anything meaningful happened in it. Pitch/velocity is a fully separate alpha-beta (g-h) predictor-corrector filter (`pitch.h`) fed a signed quarter-cycle distance on every crossing (zero otherwise) — orthogonal to bitstream validity, so pitch keeps updating even while position is unlocked. Absolute position is only trusted after `valid_counter` exceeds `VALID_BITS` (24) consecutive samples where the LFSR-predicted next bit matches the observed bit; any direction change immediately zeroes `valid_counter`, forcing a full 24-sample relock — a self-consistency-driven hysteresis/reacquisition gate, structurally similar in *purpose* to ScratchLab's four failed consecutive-window/hysteresis candidates but implemented at single-sample/single-bit granularity against a cryptographically-derived consistency check rather than a coarse window-count heuristic. ScratchLab's decoder has no absolute-position concept at all (pure relative-velocity integration, so nothing self-corrects drift), no persistent sample-level state between windows, and conflates direction/velocity/confidence into one per-window scalar computation, so when phase is ambiguous, everything derived from it is ambiguous together — there is no equivalent to xwax's independent, always-live pitch estimate.
  - Harness (built and run; **kept entirely outside this git repository**, per the licensing boundary — no xwax source or ported logic exists anywhere in the ScratchLab repo). Wrote an original C driver (not xwax source) that reads the fixtures' Float32 WAVs, converts to Int16, and feeds them sample-by-sample into an unmodified upstream 1.9 `timecoder.c`/`lut.c`/`pitch.h`, logging every direction-flag change plus periodic (every 1024 samples, matching ScratchLab's established window) pitch/valid_counter/absolute-position snapshots; compiled and run with plain `cc`/`gh api` entirely under a local scratch directory. Calibration against `steady_normal` first (per instruction to measure baseline before comparing) tried `serato_2a`/`serato_2b`/`serato_cd`/`traktor_a` × `phono` 0/1: **`serato_2b` (Serato 2nd Ed., Side B) with `phono=0` is the only configuration that achieves a sustained, genuine absolute-position lock** (valid_counter climbing into the thousands, e.g. 6,568 peak on `steady_normal`, non‑`-1` resolved positions), confirming these Rane ONE MKII hardware captures carry genuine, valid Serato NoiseMap control-vinyl content, not a generic synthetic tone — direct, decisive evidence answering "which xwax family" more precisely than the binary inspection alone could.
  - Measured across all 15 fixtures at that calibration: on steady/one-way fixtures (`steady_normal`, `slow_forward`, `slow_backward`, `fast_forward`, `fast_backward`, `position_start/middle/end`) xwax's absolute-position-gated direction stream (only trusting `forwards` while `position != -1`) is **perfectly clean — zero false direction changes in every case**, versus the small pre-existing blips ScratchLab's own decoder shows there. Critically, gated on position-lock, **`start_stop` goes from 3,923 raw per-sample direction flips to exactly 0 gated false reversals, and `needle_lift` likewise to 0** — better than every one of ScratchLab's four tested candidates, none of which reached 0 on both. But the same gate is devastating on fast technique content: `slow_reversals` retains only 16 of 47 genuine transitions (34%), `fast_reversals` 6 of 54 (11%), `fine_flicks` 0 of 84 (0%, essentially never locks — `lockedFrac`=0.022), and `baby_scratch` 0 of 307 (0%, `lockedFrac`=0.108). The raw (ungated) per-sample `forwards` signal, before any lock-gating, is even less usable on its own: it produces 3,117 flips on `fine_flicks` and 5,684 on `baby_scratch` against 84/307 genuine transitions — 20-30x noisier than genuine content, and on `start_stop` the raw flips cluster heavily in the exact same seconds (11-13s, 24-29s, 32-33s) already known from prior audits to be near-stop/hand-catch moments, not spread uniformly — consistent with xwax's fixed-amplitude zero-crossing threshold chattering against a slowly-adapting EMA baseline specifically when the encoded carrier's own frequency collapses toward near-DC at near-zero platter speed (verified this is not simply an input-gain problem: whole-file RMS is a healthy ~1,000-1,100 in 16-bit terms against a threshold of 128).
  - **Conclusion:** xwax's absolute-bitstream-lock signal is the first tested approach (audio-only or otherwise) to reach **zero** false reversals on both `start_stop` and `needle_lift` simultaneously — better than `nearStopHysteresis` (86%/no `needle_lift` coverage) and every near-zero-state candidate from the two preceding audits. But it achieves that specifically by refusing to commit to *any* direction unless 24 consecutive samples of self-consistent LFSR bits accumulate, which is exactly the property that makes it nearly blind to ScratchLab's fastest, most important technique content (`fine_flicks`/`baby_scratch`) on these particular hardware captures — a fundamentally different, and for this product, more severe trade-off than a false-positive problem: it would swap "occasionally reports a spurious reversal near a stop" for "usually reports no direction at all during real scratching." Naively wiring raw `tc.forwards` into ScratchLab without the lock gate would be **worse** than the status quo (tens of times noisier than genuine content). No candidate is recommended for direct production adoption as measured; the viable path, if pursued, is a genuine reimplementation of xwax's *architecture* (per-sample zero-crossing + orthogonal alpha-beta pitch + self-consistency-gated hysteresis) written from scratch by ScratchLab (ideas/algorithms are not copyrightable; only xwax's actual code is restricted), tuned for ScratchLab's specific signal chain and possibly loosening the 24-bit consensus requirement — a nontrivial engineering investment and Karl's product/scope decision, not a drop-in integration.

  - Completed: added `TimecodeSignedRateDenseEstimator` — a pure Swift value-type state machine that treats the stereo pair as a quadrature signal (left ≈ A cos θ, right ≈ A sin θ) and applies the standard quadrature-encoder identity (direction = crossing edge slope × other-channel sign × a fixed per-channel calibration constant, derived from d(cos θ)/dt = −sin θ·θ′ and d(sin θ)/dt = cos θ·θ′) at every zero crossing on either channel, emitting one `TimecodeSignedRateSample` per crossing — roughly twice the local carrier frequency, i.e. hundreds to thousands of samples/sec, versus the existing decoder's ~10–46 Hz per-window output. This is textbook two-phase-encoder decoding (correcting an initial draft that used only the other channel's raw sign, which recovers phase quadrant but not direction), unrelated in architecture to xwax's per-sample LFSR/Schmitt-trigger bitstream design. Cross-block state (last raw sample per channel, last crossing time per channel, and an exponential envelope tracker with a real-elapsed-time time constant rather than a per-call blend factor) persists across blocks and is proven chunking-invariant by two dedicated tests. Silence/no-signal, ambiguous-direction, implausible-frequency, non-finite, and non-monotonic/gapped input are all handled fail-closed and deterministically; a detected gap discards crossing/envelope continuity before processing the new block so no sample is ever computed from evidence spanning the gap. 12 deterministic unit tests cover every required scenario, including one that pipes dense output directly into the unmodified `TimecodeSignedRateExcursionStabilizer` offline. Measurement (production-callback-sized 4,800-frame blocks, all 15 full local hardware captures): output density is 900–6,900 samples/sec depending on fixture speed (`stationary`/`position_*`/`steady_normal` unaffected, dropping only during genuine silence — `stationary` 10.0 Hz all-`noSignal`, `needle_lift` shows 16.9s of correctly-detected no-signal duration). These specific per-fixture counts were later invalidated and superseded by a corrected-envelope rerun after a chunking-invariance bug fix (see the newest entry above for current numbers); the trade-off conclusion itself was reconfirmed, not overturned. Sweeping the threshold down (per the task's own instruction not to tune against one fixture) to `0.02` recovers partial retention (31/55 genuine commits on `fine_flicks`, 100/230 on `baby_scratch`) but reintroduces 1 false reversal on `start_stop` and 2 on `needle_lift`; at `0.01`, retention improves further (55 and 230 genuine commits) but false reversals grow to 5 (`start_stop`) and 2 (`needle_lift`). `slow_reversals`/`fast_reversals` retain genuine transitions in both directions at every threshold tested (18 and 46–53 committed reversal pairs respectively). Processing cost is 0.014–0.017× captured duration (all 15 fixtures, ~71s of audio processed in ~1.2s). **Conclusion: this is the same fundamental trade-off every prior candidate in this investigation has hit** — no tested threshold cleanly separates `start_stop`/`needle_lift`'s false near-stop evidence from genuine `fine_flicks`/`baby_scratch` content; the conservative end matches the best false-reversal result seen so far at the cost of the two hardest techniques' genuine content, and relaxing it trades that back in roughly the same proportion prior candidates showed. The estimator's own contribution is a much denser, cross-block-continuous, honestly-stated raw signal (a documented improvement in signal quality/architecture) — not a solved false-positive/genuine-retention trade-off, which remains unresolved and is not recommended for production integration as measured. No candidate is recommended for wiring into `TimecodePhaseDecoder`, `TimecodeControlPipeline`, capture, playback, notation, export, or UI.

## Deprioritized / out-of-scope (planning amendment)

Companion to the planning amendment landed in `AI_CONTEXT.md`, `docs/current_architecture_reality.md`, `docs/capture_spec_v1.md`, and `docs/product_roadmap.md`. Items below are not on any consumer milestone and should not pick up implementation effort without an explicit roadmap revision.

| Existing plan | Status after amendment | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| "Full virtual platter" framing wherever it appears | **Revise** | Replace with "teaching platter" framing. Make clear the platter alone teaches baby-scratch-class techniques. |
| Any "DJ rig in the app" / "perform in ScratchLab" marketing copy | **Revise** | Reposition as coaching. `AI_CONTEXT.md` → *What ScratchLab is — and is not* is the canonical positioning. |
| Generic "audio engine" tasks not tied to capture, notation playback, or coaching feedback | **Deprioritize** | Move to research bucket; not part of consumer milestones. |
| "Realtime live mixing" / "perform-out" features | **Deprioritize** | Pair with real DJ apps via Performer Monitor + Direct Capture instead. |
| Free-form audio synthesis from motion | **Move to experimental** | R&D bucket only. |
| Any plan that would require schema/format changes to support new techniques | **Block** | Techniques ride on target patterns, not new event types. Schema discipline preserved. |
| Practice-mode complexity beyond the four-mode crossfader picker | **Defer** | Don't add a fifth mode until the four ship and we have user data. |

## Product direction — macOS-only active production target (2026-08-12)

> **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-29.** This retirement was undone in code: `1061dc33` (2026-08-15,
> "Restore ScratchLab iOS and iPad production target") and `37f81c06` (2026-08-22, "Restore
> watchOS companion motion capture target"). At repository baseline `baa06fc9` the iOS `ScratchLab` target,
> the `ScratchLabWatch` target, and `ScratchLab.xcscheme` are all present. See
> "Product direction — multiplatform (2026-08-29)" below. This block is kept for history only.

- [x] Retire ScratchLab iOS and ScratchLabWatch as active production Xcode targets
  - Files: `ScratchLab.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj`, `ScratchLab.xcodeproj/xcshareddata/xcschemes/ScratchLab.xcscheme` (removed), `TASKS.md`, `DEV_LOG.md`
  - Done when: the `ScratchLab` (iOS) and `ScratchLabWatch` (watchOS) `PBXNativeTarget`s, their build phases/build-files/configuration lists/product references/target dependency, and the combined `ScratchLab.xcscheme` are removed from the active project graph; `ScratchLabDesktop`/`ScratchLabDesktopTests`/`ScratchLabDatasetBuilder` and all shared source are unaffected; every iOS/watchOS-exclusive Swift file remains on disk (unregistered from any target, still browsable/restorable) for potential future lightweight Companion Camera / sensor companion work; macOS Capture/Practice/Review continue to require no iPhone or Apple Watch; and `scripts/build.sh mac` plus the authoritative macOS test suite pass from a fresh checkout.

**ScratchLab's active production product is now macOS-only.** The former iOS/watchOS implementations remain preserved in source/history for potential future lightweight Companion Camera / sensor companion work.

## Product direction — multiplatform (2026-08-29)

- [x] Reaffirm ScratchLab as a multiplatform product across macOS, iOS/iPadOS, and watchOS
  - Context: the 2026-08-12 "macOS-only" retirement above was reversed in code — `1061dc33` (2026-08-15) restored the iOS/iPad production target and `37f81c06` (2026-08-22) restored the `ScratchLabWatch` target. At repository baseline `baa06fc9` those targets and `ScratchLab.xcscheme` are present, and the active branch `feature/ios-capture-camera-ux` is iOS/iPadOS/watchOS work.
  - Decision (2026-08-29): ScratchLab remains multiplatform. `PROFILE.md`, `README.md`, `AI_CONTEXT.md`, `AGENTS.md`, and `docs/**` already reflect this and are unchanged.
  - Reconciliation (docs only): this record + the SUPERSEDED banner above; a new 2026-08-29 entry in `DEV_LOG.md`; `AI_HANDOFF.md` BLOCKER → RESOLVED; the `AI_HANDOFF/next_prompt.md` stop-gate cleared. No Swift, project-graph, scheme, or WIP change.
